Publications Center for Peace Studies www.studiperlapace.it The bodies of the technical-industrial area of Defense (35 Establishments and Arsenals) before the reform (law 28
December 1995, n. 549) constituted a legacy that Defense had carried with it since the time of the First World War.
At the time they represented production entities which, over the years, essentially became maintenance entities. Of these, 24 were from the Army, 9 from the Navy while 3 had a distinctly joint force character; the Air Force has never had factories or arsenals as it has always used the companies and civil companies producing the carriers for production, as well as for 4th level maintenance.

INDUSTRIAL TECHNICAL AREA 80


The plants in tables "A" and "B" have moved into the technical operational area in application of the specific rule of the law on the reform of top management (law 18 February 1997, n. 25), which attributes the direct responsibility of the Chiefs of Staff of the Armed Forces to maintain the efficiency of the military instrument entrusted to them.
• possible transformation into joint-stock companies of the reconverted and economically convenient entities, which are destined to become an industrial area not of the Armed Forces, with the task of operating both in favor of the CEO and in extemal sectors.
From this perspective, with the aim of ensuring unitary private management for the latter entities, the Defense Industries Agency was established (article 22 of the legislative decree 30.7.1999, n° 300), which must operate in compliance with the objective of economic management and the principles that regulate competition and the market.
From this process, sanctioned by the legislative decree of 28 November 1997, n. 459, the subdivision of the military establishments is derived in tables "A" (5 maintenance centers for the Army), "B" (3 arsenals and 1 joint missile maintenance center for the Navy), "C " (19 Army bodies and 4 Navy bodies), "D" (including the technical centers and aircraft maintenance departments of the operational technical area), referred to in Ministerial Decree 20.1.1998, implementing the aforementioned legislative decree .
In this context, the industrial area (which concems only the Army and the Navy) has been subjected to a profound reform, aimed above all at framing the actual needs of technical logistical support of the Armed Forces through the identification of a limited and efficient number of Maintenance Hubs, also responding to operational and strategic requirements, while the remaining factories/arsenals have passed through the area of the Secretary General for the analysis of their production capacity, in order to identify other possible production sectors, foreseeing their decommissioning in case of a negative outcome of the aforementioned analysis.

The Agency is based in Rome, has a legal personality under public law with activities subject to civil law regulations; and is endowed with administrative, organisational, financial and accounting autonomy and has the assets of the various units necessary for the management of the activities. • In particular, the pursuit of economic management occurs through a reduction in costs and an increase in revenues.
The reduction in costs will be determined by the rationalization of activities, the increase in efficiency and the transfer of costs, currently related to production or plant support, to production activities through the reconversion and optimization of personnel. The objective of the reconversion is to bring at least 50% of the staff to directly productive activities and the remaining 50% to production support activities and plant services. This will allow a percentage increase in production costs compared to support and factory costs, at the same time as a reduction in overall costs linked to the increase in efficiency and the redefinition of the overall personnel employed. • The total number of employees in these factories is currently around 1500 people and the industrial result for 2000 is highly negative, as the costs (around 100 billion) are far higher than the value of the production developed. ____________ In the specific field of products, for ammunition factories, the start of new production lines for military armaments and the expansion of the activities of propellants, cordage and chemical-pharmaceutical products to the competitive commercial market are envisaged.

Currently, the Italian defense industry includes private companies such as FIAT (vehicles, space, propulsion and electronics), MARCONI (electronics and communications), ELETTRONICA (electronic warfare), MACCHI (trainer aircraft), BERETTA (small and medium weapons caliber), INTERMARINE (naval construction) and state-owned companies such as FINMECCANICA (aerospace, helicopters, defense electronics, energy and transport) and FINCANTIERI (shipbuilding). It has a total of 30,000 employees, records a tumover of 4,500 million euros with 35% exports and ensures approximately 80% of national defense needs. These companies have already concluded the financial reorganization and industrial relaunch plan, concentrating intemally all the major skills of the sector (industrial centers specialized in particular product lines) and rationalizing and optimizing the company structures.

4.5.1 WEAG (Westem European Armaments Group) This is an optimization process which involves, alongside the industries, various departments and sectors of the Public Administration in an action to coordinate the activities to direct them towards common objectives.
These alliances have also been supported by extensive govemment collaboration and, in particular, Defense has defined cooperation agreements aimed at creating those synergies essential for strengthening the defense industrial sector.
The initiatives aimed at creating a European armaments market are mainly the WEAG (Westem European Armament Group), the OCCAR (Organisme Conjoint de Cooperation en matière d'Armament) and the LoI (Letter of Intent).
Italy is fully included in this process following the agreements signed in April 2001 between Finmeccanica, EADS and Bae Systems in the missile sector and those in June 2000 between Finmeccanica and EADS in the aeronautical field, preceded by the agreements already operational between Alenia and Marconi Systems, in defense electronics, and between Agusta and GKN in the helicopter sector.
On this basis, for some time now we have been trying to promote the creation of a real European defense industry, attenuating protectionist barriers within EU countries and encouraging sectoral mergers of industries, in order to concentrate the various national capabilities in poles of great production quality. mergers aimed at establishing transnational production hubs for each production sector, of such consistency and technological level as to ensure their presence on the world armaments market.
In the European multilateral context, the desire of the European Union (EU) to create a European Security and Defense Identity has required collaboration to create a technological and industrial base aimed at allowing the creation of an integrated and interdependent industrial system at a European level, without which the greater operational autonomy sought would lose concreteness and become unrealistic. ____________

The possession of legal personality, which gives the Organization its own negotiating capacity (stipulation of contracts, hiring of staff, conduct of negotiation activities in general), allows OCCAR to manage said programs completely autonomously, without having to resort to the application of the national procedures regarding acquisition policy.
The downsizing of the WEU and the absorption of its functions by the EU seem to mark the days of life of the Group, but the need to have a political forum in Europe, directed by the Defense Ministers, in which the possibility of discussing together and developing cooperative activities makes maintaining the Group's activity essential, at least for the short/medium term.
The OCCAR (FR, GE, IT, UK) was established with the aim of rationalizing the "demand" through a common policy on the procurement of armaments, in order to guarantee interoperability between the respective Armed Forces and achieve economies of scale in the sector. From this perspective, the Organization's task is to coordinate, control and implement the programs relating to armaments that are assigned to it by the Member States, thus improving the effectiveness of the management of cooperation projects, in terms of cost, time and performance.
The WEAG's decision-making activity takes place on a unanimous basis and consequently, taking into account the large number of participating countries and the many different national interests to be considered, it has not led to concrete results, except those - quite limited - in the Research sector and Technology.

Some of the management rigidities inherent to the EUCLID MOU and the Eurofinder procedure, which bound the WEAG countries to complete mutual openness regarding participation in each program, have recently been overcome with the signing of the Europe MOU. WEAG has a structure, the Research Cell, based in Brussels, specifically dedicated to the technical-administrative management of contracts which are mainly assigned to defense industries. WEAG research is essentially conducted in an industrial context, has an annual budget (which each country individually and in a differentiated manner prepares in its own budget) and provides for a financial participation of the industry of the order of 50%. The projects are identified according to a "Top down" procedure (EUCLID MOU) or "bottom up" through the Eurofinder procedure which provides for the free and spontaneous presentation of individual projects by industries and/or research bodies interested in conducting them (and co-financing them ).

Some difficulties encountered by the CNAD in responding to the needs of member countries, towards the mid-1980s, led the National Armaments Directors of France, Germany, Great Britain and the United States of America to establish the so-called "Four Power NADs ", which became "Five Power NADs" in April 2000, with the entry of Italy. This is an informal partnership intended to coordinate the positions of the major nations and to define common strategies regarding the major intemational cooperation programs.
The USA, in fact, facilitated by the unitary legislative context and by the advantage of operating in a single intemal market and with a single customer, have been able to express uniqueness and coherence in defense and foreign policy strategies, with the consequent possibility of operating in the world market with great competitiveness and effectiveness.
4.6. Intemational Industrial Landscape The changes that have occurred in the last decade in the intemational geostrategic scenario have been addressed by Westem countries not only on a political level but also on an industrial level.
They have therefore quickly achieved alliances and mergers between the large national industrial complexes, among which it is worth mentioning Lockheed Martin, Northrop Grumman for the aerospace, missile and defense electronics sector, Boeing for aerospace and Raytheon for missile sector.
The most evident manifestation of this desire dates back to the beginning of 1998 with the launch of a study aimed at the creation of a European Aerospace and Defense Company (EADC), through the integration of the At a bilateral level, the Italian Defense has so far signed 48 Agreements (14 already ratified) and 12 are under negotiation.
Among these, those relating to the countries of Eastem Europe (in particular Russia), North Africa (Egypt and Algeria), the Far East (India, China, South Korea, Singapore and Malaysia) and the South are of great interest. America (Chile). The ratification and operationalization of these Agreements will, in short, prevent the long times usually required for the approval process from being perceived abroad as indifference to the problems of cooperation.
Even the major industrialized countries of Europe, albeit with a certain difficulty, have subsequently implemented specific rationalizations on a national basis and have also attempted to establish alliances and mergers between their companies in the defense sector. ____________ The US defense industry was the first to react to this new and unexpected reference framework. (Fig. 1) In the context of intemational relations for cooperation in the armaments sector, bilateral govemment agreements, which are based on specific MoUs, assume particular importance. The formalization of these agreements responds to strategic needs and, at the same time, offers industries better possibilities for intervention in the areas of interest. The forms of collaboration generally envisaged in this type of agreement range from the purchase/sale of products and/or production licenses, to joint study, development and production.
In this context, the participating Nations have also established the "Intemational Cooperative Opportunities Group (ICOG)" which aims to strengthen transatlantic cooperation in the field of defense systems, with the aim of identifying cooperation opportunities and developing the more concrete ones.
Publications Center for Peace Studies www.studiperlapace.it Faced with a market characterized by production redundancies, strong competitiveness and reduced demand, a review process was launched which led industries to carry out a courageous and demanding action of restructuring, rationalization and concentration of resources in areas of excellence, allowing them to guarantee an adequate presence and competitiveness in the armaments market.

This process of industrial reorganization is constantly evolving and is leading, as mentioned, to the creation of mergers and amalgamations aimed at the creation of transnational production centers for each production sector, of such consistency and technological level as to ensure the presence on the armaments market, such as ASTRIUM (EADS, BAe Systems) in the space sector, Eurocopter (EADS) and Agusta Westland in the helicopter sector, MBDA (BAe Systems, Aerospatiale Matra and Alenia Difesa) in the missile field, Alenia Marconi Systems (BAe Systems and Alenia Difesa) in the defense electronics, while an agreement is being finalized between Finmeccanica and EADS for the establishment of an equal joint venture which will control the activities of Alenia, the German DASA and the Spanish CASA.
In parallel with this process, the path of intemational cooperation was pursued by creating numerous collaborations, through consortia, companies and joint ventures, relating to specific programs. requirements and related financial charges; • official attribution of the mandate to the competent DG; • general indication, by the DG, of the technical-financial-industrial parameters which In particular, national vertical integration operations have been carried out, such as the mergers between the French companies Aerospatiale and Matra Hautes Technologies and between the English companies BAe and GEC (Marconi Electronic System), transnational sectoral acquisition operations, as in the case of the German DASA towards the Spanish CASA, and merger operations of companies of different nationalities (Aerospatiale Matra with DASA/CASA) which gave rise to the European Aeronautic Defense and Space Company (EADS), the first European aerospace group. (Fig. 2) The technical-operational area is responsible for technical planning and programming, which takes shape with the definition of the objectives of strengthening and modemizing the instrument and essentially develops through the following fundamental phases: • identification of needs; • definition of the technical-operational characteristics of the means and materials and evaluation of the major European defense companies; the ambitious project proved difficult to implement and in its place, sectoral production hubs were established through transnational structural agreements.

Once activated, the General Management identifies the purchasing methods by determining the scope of the contractor's search (national, Community, GATT, WEAG or between countries signatory to particular intemational agreements) and the type of procedure to be adopted (bidding, private negotiation, competitive tender, etc.).
4.7.3 Advisory Committee In order to streamline the procedural process relating to Defense contracts, the recent legislative decree of 28 December 1998, n. 496 established an "Advisory Committee", chaired by the Secretary General of Defense, with the task of expressing "opinions" on the draft contracts deriving from intemational cooperation agreements on armaments and on those implementing programs approved by law or decree of the Minister of Defense pursuant to the aforementioned law 436/88. The Committee's competence concems programs with amounts exceeding 5 million euros.
These opinions concem the technical, administrative and economic profiles as well as the adequacy and convenience of the estimated prices to be based on the tenders (restricted procedures), or agreed with the contracting companies (negotiated procedure).
Said supply orders consequently materialize in contractual relationships between the CEO and the private contractors according to the national and community procedures in force.
The technical-administrative area (competent General Directorates) is interested in the acquisition of supplies through procurement orders for goods and/or services by the General Staff.
Furthermore, in contracts stipulated by private negotiation, as a further form of control and adjustment of prices to a principle of congruity, the "safeguard clause" is inserted, by virtue of which the company must provide insurance that the price offered does not is higher than what it has practiced, in relation to similar services, towards its best customer, even at an intemational level, with the clarification that the violation of this clause will lead to the reduction of the contract conditions within the aforementioned limit.
The Minister of Defense reports annually to the competent Parliamentary Commissions on the aforementioned programs and on the contractual activity resulting from them.
For this purpose, the General Secretariat of Defense has prepared a specific "Methodology for determining company hourly costs for services or supplies" which indicates the "guidelines" as well as the procedures to be followed in order to avoid, as also noted by the same Court of Auditors, that the so-called "costs of inefficiency" of the industrial system fall on the Defense Administration.
In this case, and provided that the programs do not refer to the maintenance of equipment or the replenishment of stocks, the opinion of the competent Parliamentary Commissions must be obtained before issuing the ministerial decree.
This control is carried out by a Commission specifically appointed by the contracting body which, in order to express its assessments, makes use of the results of checks previously carried out by another Commission at the requested company, aimed at ascertaining the impact of all the company costs in running the business, in order to determine the hourly rate of labor which will constitute one of the elements for the formation of the price of the good to be acquired. ____________ While with the adoption of "restricted procedures" (private tender and competitive tender), the supply is based on the price previously identified by the Administration following the definition of specific "technical conditions" and the market situation, with the procedures "negotiate" arises the need to subject the price offered by the company chosen for the negotiation to a particular form of control called "technical-economic congruity analysis".
This is due to the frequency, particularly in the field of WARLIKE materials, of resorting to the purchase of goods whose production is guaranteed by industrial property rights, due to the nature of which it is not possible to promote the competition for public offers, which a single company can supply with the technical requirements and the required degree of perfection.

Despite this regulatory deficiency, and even before the issuance of the aforementioned simplifying rule, since December 1998 the General Directorate of the Commissariat and General Services has included on a specific website not only all notices and tender notices relating to its activity, but also the related specifications and directives which regulate in a precise and timely manner the procedures and criteria with which the Administration itself is required to comply in its activity.
In current intemational commercial practice, it is customary to pay the supplier an advance on the supply price at the time of signing the contract. This practice is not compatible with the general accounting rules of the State according to which it is legitimate to pay the price exclusively after the supply has been carried out (art. 12 LCGS, art. 5, paragraph 1 Legislative Decree 28 March 1977, no. 79 converted, with amendments, by law 28 May 1997, no. 140).
Despite the presence of intrinsic difficulties in operating with the timing often required by operational needs, the negotiation activity of the Defense Administration is based on the principles established in Legislative Decree 3
February 1993, n. 29 as amended by Legislative Decree no. 80/98, of cost-effectiveness, effectiveness, efficiency, in one with the aim of ensuring maximum transparency in carrying out procedures. In this context, it should be highlighted that in the context of law 340/2000, which lays down rules on administrative simplification, in order to give practical implementation to the aforementioned need for transparency, it has been established that all public administrations are required to publish on one or more IT sites, identified with Prime Ministerial Decree, notices and tender notices. These forms of communication do not, however, replace the other advertising obligations provided for by Community legislation, including the obligations of publication in newspapers or periodicals.

4.7.5 Computerization of contractual and "e-procurement" procedures Particular attention was also paid to strengthening the Public Relations Office, which, through an intranet system connected to all intemal offices and Divisions, allows us to guarantee, in real time, the satisfaction of the information requested by the various users. 4.7.4 Peculiarities relating to the armaments market The armaments market is a highly specialized market where companies operate that adopt solutions with very high technological content. Therefore, the areas of provenance of the weapon systems and their components and spare parts are very limited. It is inevitable, therefore, for Italy too to rely on the foreign market for its supplies, in particular the US one.
FA, provided for by numerous pre-existing laws, the activation of which often caused considerable delays in the start of the contractual activity due to the recurring overlapping of the relevant competences.
In this regard, it is worth mentioning the directives relating to the "verification of the adequacy of abnormally low offers", the "contractual execution" and the "procedures for the testing of materials" of the commissionerate which, again for the purposes of widespread diffusion, are were also published in the Official Gazette of the Italian Republic.
In fact, national legislation does not take into account the financial reality of the intemational markets of the sector, limiting the possibility of negotiating and acquiring materials produced abroad.
In this context, and after having consulted the Council of State on the matter, a draft provision was proposed for inclusion in the finance law for 2002 which contemplates the possibility, in the cases described above, of granting advances of part of the contract price, subject to the provision of a suitable guarantee, also in derogation of the current provisions which prohibit it. ninety two


The procedures in force for the issuing of authorizations for the export of military equipment and the transfer of the related documentation are currently regulated by law 185/90 and related implementing regulation Prime Ministerial Decree of 25 September 1999, n. 448. The regulations and controls provided for by this law are among the most restrictive among those currently in force in other Westem countries.
4.9 Research, development and acquisition In the last 15 years, in particular, there has been a real proliferation of intemational cooperation programs for the creation of vehicles and materials and, at the same time, an increase in acquisition programs for systems interforce value. This trend has allowed significant reductions in the costs of individual acquisition and modemization programs with a consequent beneficial impact on the defense investment sector.
This was possible thanks to the increasingly marked intemationalization of both Italian and European defense industries; to the decisive impulse recently given to the European integration process in many fields, including the Defense sector, through legal and regulatory initiatives (OCCAR Agency, Letter of
4.8 Marketing of weapons The peculiarity of the Defense organization and the need to immediately satisfy operational needs that are sometimes not programmable in detail, in sectors that do not allow continuity of services, would recommend providing specific exemptions for the AD in relation to certain types of materials which, although commonly acquired, for the FF.AA. they could take on a strategic character (for example, fuel lubricants).
Other operational adjustments to the authorization procedures remain to be examined to allow the national industry to be able to present itself at its best in the process of European structural integration of the armaments industry and participate, on an equal basis, in co-production programmes.
The need to rationalize and optimize state spending on goods and services that are common to all administrations has led to the establishment of a specific company within the Ministry of Economy and Finance (CONSIP SpA) which, following of specific "economic tenders", proceeds with the stipulation of specific agreements for goods and services, which each administration has the obligation to adhere to in order to satisfy its own needs. This, in addition to the achievement of economies of scale, leads to a streamlining of acquisition procedures by individual administrations, with consequent advantages also in terms of lower use of human resources.
The opportunity of ratifying the Framework Agreement, signed by the Defense Ministers of Italy, France, England, Germany, Spain and Sweden in Famborough on 27 July 2000, was therefore taken to make some necessary adjustments. contractor while guaranteeing the necessary "level playing field" as well as the secrecy, where required, and the transparency of all tender procedures.
However, more than ten years after the entry into force of this Law, several points have emerged that require modifications, in order to streamline the procedures without eliminating controls. In particular, its inadequacy in managing the complex phenomenon of European industrial concentration has been noted, with the formation of new companies in the form of Transnational Defense Companies (STDs), which presupposes the possibility of rationalizing and specializing production units according to a logic of industrial efficiency, through the circulation of parts produced between different factories located in different countries. ____________ As regards DUAL-USE material, export is regulated by Legislative Decree 89/97 and takes into account regulation 1334/2000 of the European Community. The entire investigation is carried out by the Ministry of Productive Activities which grants the export authorization after presentation to another Consultative Committee composed of representatives of the MAE (President), Defense, the Interior, Productive Activities, the Treasury, the Healthcare, Education and Research and the Customs Agency.
Furthermore, the Defense General Staff, after having asked the Armed Forces General Staff and the General Secretariat of Defence/DNA for information on the material under examination, expresses a binding opinion for the security and military technical aspects.
Publications Center for Peace Studies www.studiperlapace.it After an initial preliminary phase, conducted by the SMD or the MAE in relation to the type of negotiation, the request for authorization is normally brought to the attention of an Advisory Committee, made up of representatives of the MAE (President), the Defence, the Interior, Productive Activities, Environment and Customs Agency, which expresses a non-binding opinion. In the case of classified material, during the investigation phase the ANS-UCSi is asked for its opinion and possible authorization for the disclosure of classified information.
In summary, the bureaucratic process involves the request for authorization for commercial negotiations and, upon conclusion of a contract, a subsequent request for export.

In the intemational field, Defense's activity and commitment have manifested themselves in an even more sensitive manner due to the dense network of relationships that characterizes the sector at a European and global level. The traditional areas (WEAG, NATO) have been dynamically joined by new ones (Loi, ETAP, etc.), helping to outline a rapidly and extremely competitively evolving framework. oriented primarily towards the aeronautical sector and currently being defined, will commit huge human and financial resources, currently estimated in the order of 20 billion/year (10.33 million) per country for the next Furthermore, Defense actively participated in the study conducted by in preparation of the National Research Plan (PNR) which, starting from the observation of the existence of a "gap" between the Italian, European and, even more so, that of the USA and Japanese, envisaged a series of strategic actions (in particular: a structural action for the strategic repositioning of the national scientific system, an action in the short-medium term for the recovery of efficiency and effectiveness of the intervention tools and an action of a transversal for monitoring and evaluating interventions) and identified the financial resources deemed necessary. The technological priorities identified by the PNR, however, referring to the totality of scientific disciplines, involve only marginally, and always on a "dual" basis, the interests of the Defense which will therefore have to continue to develop and finance research of military interest on its own.
At the national level, the launch of the first national military research program was promoted, which led to the collection of over 228 new research projects relating to the sectors and priorities identified by the Defense General Staff (Directive SMD 004). Among these projects, which cover almost all the technological sectors, the development of which is considered essential to ensure a qualified national presence in the context of future armament programmes, 30 have already been selected, the overall cost of which is estimated to be in the order of the 45 billion, financed in the financial year 2001 or the following.
Following a Franco-British initiative, ETAP was launched with the aim of identifying and jointly conducting those technological research programs that will guarantee the availability of the technologies that the two countries, individually, deem necessary to support their specific programs aircraft of the future. Germany, Sweden, Portugal and Italy were subsequently added to the initial nucleus of France and Great Britain (reconstituting the same group of LOI countries within the ETAP framework). These research programs, In parallel with the attribution, at a legislative level (law on military leaders), to the Secretary General and DNA of the responsibility relating to the development of the sector, the activities already underway were strengthened and new ones were launched in the national and intemational fields.
4.9.5.2 ETAP program (European Technology Acquisition Programme) applications. It is therefore considered necessary to seek maximum synergy between the needs of studies which, although originating from needs linked to different systems or applications, involve identical technological bases; - move towards using all the intellectual and organizational resources available in the country, involving universities, industries and public and private research institutions without limits or distinctions: - considerably increase the financial resources to be allocated to research, pursuing the ultimate goal of aligning national spending with that of the European countries with which Italy aspires to compete.
Italy ensures the contribution of its experts to over 60 working groups, financing their participation. The study activities, however, do not provide specific funding but are conducted on a voluntary and free basis by experts made available by the Alliance countries who selectively decide which studies to take part in and contribute to. The driving force and value of this commitment must therefore be sought in the exchange of information that the network of govemmental and academic experts from the Alliance countries who report to the RTO is able to ensure, since there are no funds dedicated to contracts to assign. The "product" of the Organization, study reports, scientific publications, symposia, etc., is of the highest level and continues to constitute a point of reference for the entire scientific and technological community of NATO countries.

Publications Center for Peace Studies www.studiperlapace.it is also necessary to invest more in research and development, thus promoting the reduction of the technological and operational "gap" of European military instruments compared to those across the Atlantic.
With pragmatism and gradualness and with the inevitable setbacks, the member countries of the Union have been able to "communitarianize" large sectors of activity that were once typically national, starting with those of an economic and then monetary nature. There have therefore been significant transfers of sovereignty in the various aspects of free circulation and, ultimately, of money.
However, in the area of civilian personnel, professional development must be pursued, facilitating a significant leap in mentality, both at a military and civilian level, to enhance the component of civilian Defense personnel who constitute a very important resource of the military instrument as a whole.
At the same time, however, it appears appropriate and convenient, taking into account the realities of the resources that Europeans dedicate to the defense sector, to seek a healthy and mutually convenient relationship of cooperation with the US defense industry. The Europe of Defense cannot and must not pay a systematic toll, to have those modem operating systems that it needs, to possible logics and sirens of productive autarky, isolating itself from a healthy pressure of competition, in this case predominantly American, and by the positive effects that this competition - in terms of prices and quality - has on European products. It should not be overlooked that, in some specific sectors, the United States has highly sophisticated know-how, which Europe cannot ignore.
Italy, together with other European countries, is pursuing a defense materials policy along a political-operational and industrial path of extensive intemational co-production.
This work of reorganization and modemization of our military instrument, in a European and Atlantic perspective, raises the question of the resources to be allocated to the sector.
From all this arose the need for an armaments policy that is as unitary and coordinated as possible to provide the European Security and Defense Policy (ESDP) with concreteness and effective tools.
In terms of personnel, the available human resources, both military and civilian, do not allow us to adequately cope with the organizational structures and require rationalization in the sense of joint forces and a more effective allocation of competence which today in some areas present some redundancy and a not always clear division of attributions. ____________ The various European bodies, which for the moment co-exist from the WEAG (Westem European Armament Group) up to the OCCAR (Joint Armaments Cooperation Organism) or the structure following the signing of the LOL ("Letter of Intent") will therefore need to be harmonised. ") to finally arrive at the appropriate changes to the EU institutional framework called upon to manage this matter.
To increase European and national strategic capabilities, it will be necessary to give greater impetus to industrial investments, strengthen the Defense-Industry connection, giving more prominence to the joint effort of the institutions and the production sector.
The nascent European dimension of security and defense passes through the pursuit of ever greater effectiveness of military spending, with a view to real planning and standardization of European operational requirements and capabilities, also through the reorganization of the European defense industry.

• Volunteers on short notice (VFB) Publications Center for Peace Studies www.studiperlapace.it The selection and recruitment procedures have been made very simple. For example, in the Army, the selection takes place on the basis of the psycho-physical and aptitude requirements possessed by the candidates, the places available in the Departments, to be filled with said type of personnel, and the date and time of submission of the application.
As regards the recruitment of this type of volunteers, the system identified responds to extremely simple criteria and is aimed at allowing the citizen to be able to carry out the service, if suitable, in a short time from the date of submission of the application (approximately one month ) and, within the limit of available places and depending on the Regiments to be fed, within the chosen Regiment. In practice, all citizens not yet in arms, in arms or already discharged, aged between 17 and 28, can be enlisted as VFAs. Applications for enlistment must be submitted to: the Military District to which they belong or to the command of the Regiment chosen to carry out the service, for young people not yet in arms or discharged; The relevant department, for military conscripts. For this category of personnel, the military service performed is not counted as useful service for carrying out the annual voluntary service.

Publications Center for Peace Studies www.studiperlapace.it The figure of the VSP, as mentioned, was established in 1995 with the same provision that established the VFBs (Legislative Decree no. 196/95 and subsequent amendments/integrations). With this provision, therefore, a "communicating system" was designed, from the bottom up, according to which the VSPs can be drawn exclusively from the VFBs (fig. n.3), which have requested them and have provided service without demerit for at least two years in the FA in which they ask to be enrolled.
These activities include: • a cultural pre-selection, at middle school level (minimum qualification required); • health checks aimed at verifying possession of the required physical requirements; • psycho-aptitude assessments.
In summary, the recruitment system adopted is a "communicating" system, so to speak, which, by providing for transits/transfers, not only for the role of VSPs, but also for the roles of Sergeants and Warrant Officers, creates a sort of osmosis between roles, which avoids the dispersion of the experience gained by the VFBs during their period of inactivity, allows its profitable use to the advantage of the FAs and allows, at the same time, to materialize a meritocratic system, through which it becomes possible for a volunteer aspire to improve one's "status".
At the moment, however, as has already been said, it has not been possible to entirely satisfy the need for volunteer recruitment with "preferential" competitions, pursuant to Presidential Decree no. 332/97. This has, therefore, led to the adoption of more rapid complementary competitive procedures, which provide for selection only in the single FA context, and are effective for the functionality of the FF.AA. same. Furthermore, these procedures offer employment guarantees that are still uncertain.
• Volunteers on permanent duty (VSP) At the moment, the possibilities of entering the world of work reserved for young people who carry out voluntary military service are connected to the completion of the expected service (currently three years - VFB, in the near future five years - VFP). These possibilities are: o short-term admission ranking; o aptitude and performance during the service carried out in the short term; o moral and cultural qualities; o outcome of education, specialization or qualification courses; o number and type of specializations/qualifications obtained; o educational qualification and/or professional qualification held.

With this measure, in fact, the pre-existing unified role was divided into
two separate roles:
• Sergeants role; • Role of Marshals; at the same time modifying the rules of recruitment, legal status and advancement.
The role of Sergeants constitutes the natural career progression of the personnel of the role of Troop Volunteers in permanent service, from which they are exclusively nourished through intemal competition and passing a specific professional refresher course.
At the same time as the remodulation of the rules on postponement, further rules are introduced regarding exemption from the draft in favor of those young people already effectively introduced into the world of work and/or attending particular post-graduate university courses, namely: incorporation: two per month, fortnightly at the 4 Leva Incorporation Centers (CIL), responsible for carrying out only incorporation operations and training activities aimed at giving the conscript a first "military" appearance. or holders of a scholarship or research grant for graduates, lasting at least one year in the case of attending a research doctorate at legally recognized universities.
With the transition to "professional", 2004 will be the last year of compulsory military service for Italian citizens belonging to the classes of 1985 and earlier. Until that date, despite the general, progressive decrease in the annual revenue from the "Draft", recruitment will be subject to appropriate "shock absorbers" such as the regionalization of military service (which will primarily affect personnel residing within 100 km of the place of assignment ) or, if this is not possible, the right to reimbursement of travel expenses. On the other hand, to safeguard the functionality and efficiency of the military instrument in this delicate and sensitive phase of transformation, more restrictive conditions have been introduced for the purposes of achieving delays in the call-up to arms for study reasons, to avoid, for example, that the enrollment at university alone constitutes a reason for delay and consequently, de facto, exemption from military service. In practice, in the two/three years preceding the suspension of the draft, those who have benefited from the deferment in previous years will be called to arms (the "natural" call to arms of the class of 1985 would take place no later than the first months of 2004 ). from military service, but in line with the principle of giving priority to those who are already in the world of work, the Minister of Defense with his own decree has the power to extend exemption from military service to them. ____________ those who are directly responsible for running a business or economic activity for at least one year or for a business started with the support provided by institutions or public bodies regarding the promotion of youth entrepreneurship and self-employment;

5.2.4 The recruitment of auxiliary officers This category, introduced by enabling law 331/2000, is intended to replace in the future the reserve officers who, being linked to compulsory conscription, will no longer be able to be recruited starting from 2006. This category, specifically regulatory provision, is not included in the professional model. It includes: • first-appointed or re-appointed reserve officers in service who will continue to be recruited from among young people subject to military service (and therefore bom before 1985), for the performance of the traditional tasks assigned to this category of personnel ; • the supplementary pilot officers on twelve-year terms, to which Italian citizens will be able to access according to the methods already in force (direct recruitment through public competition), necessary for the functionality of the FA as they are destined almost exclusively and continuously for the operational line, not being subject to the obligations of Command and attendance of refresher courses provided for officers of normal roles;

5.2.8 Female military service Since the Second World War, and with greater frequency since the early 1970s, there have been numerous legislative proposals that resonate between the Senate and the Chamber of Deputies; they all take inspiration from two articles of the Constitution which read: art.
3 “All citizens have equal social dignity and are equal before the law, without distinctions of sex, race, language, religion, political opinions, personal and social conditions”; art. 52 “The defense of the homeland is a sacred duty of the citizen. Military service is compulsory within the limits and in the manner established by law. Its fulfillment does not prejudice the citizen's employment position or the exercise of political rights. The organization of the FF.AA. is informed by the democratic spirit of the Republic". It is interesting to highlight how, from the beginning and in whatever form they were formulated, these bills were presented by representatives of all political orientations. • Since then, the numbers have risen to the current 12,000 units (not included in the organic volumes and assigned by budget law) for which, with the

The military chaplain is a Catholic priest suitable to carry out this service, appointed by Decree of the President of the Republic upon designation of the Military Ordinary and proposal of the Minister of Defense (art. 16, 17, 18 law 512, 1 June '61; art. 78, 80 First Synod of the Military Ordinariate Church). The service of the military chaplain cannot ignore complete integration with the realities of the Armed Forces. In fact, it is practiced "among" soldiers called to an increasingly advanced and complex professionalism and destined to act in national and intemational contexts. The Chaplain follows his soldiers, adapts to their conditions, participates in their missions. Its presence indicates the possibility of faithfully living the deepest ideals of the human person: honesty, coherence, discipline, fortitude, altruism, ability to serve, which are just some of the characteristics that accompany the life and professionalism of men and women in arms.
With the law on conscientious objection (L. n.230/98), the regulation of conscientious objection was radically modified.
The most significant innovations were: the recognition of conscientious objection as a "subjective right" of the citizen and the transfer of management to the National Office for the Civil Service (UNSC), within the Presidency of the Council of Ministers. Moreover, the time limit 1997-1999; the celebration of the Military Jubilee on 20 November 2000, to indicate the attention that the Church pays to the military world.
2000.
• the publication of the Apostolic Constitution "Spiritualis Militum Curae" of 21 April 1986; • the preparation and celebration of the First Synod of the Military Ordinariate Church in Italy over the years In the same ecclesial context, facts and documents have made it possible to delve deeper into the meaning and value
of this service. Just quote:
5.2.10 Conscientious objection Conscientious objection, bom as a refusal of military service and has now become a citizen's right, has had clear repercussions on the military organization given the identical target group, the equality of the civil service for the purposes of fulfillment of the military obligations and the conditions connected with the different conditions of performance of the two services (essentially, in the absence of solid moral and religious convictions, the less onerous service is chosen by the personnel subject to the military service, other conditions being equal ). This caused an exponential expansion of the phenomenon of conscientious objection from an entity of a few thousand individuals in the early '80s and to a few tens of thousands in the early '90s (effects due to jurisprudential interventions that led to equalization of the duration of civil service to that of military service), to the over 70,000 applications in 1998, to the approximately 108,000 applications in 1999 and to the 62,524 of the year Spiritual Assistance in the Italian Armed Forces is entrusted to approximately 200 Catholic Chaplains under the guidance of the Military Ordinary Archbishop. In recent years, the service has undergone various transformations to adequately cope with the new needs arising from changes in the Armed Forces. • Attention to the training of Chaplains has recently resulted in the establishment of a School for Chaplain Students, located in the Military City of Cecchignola. In the current year, twenty-five young people have begun their studies to become military chaplains under the guidance of a team of priests who take care of their training from both a human, spiritual and physical point of view. A fundamental step in the preparation of the future Chaplain is represented by the acquisition of knowledge relating to the structure of the organization of the Armed Forces from both a theoretical and practical point of view. This objective is also achieved through visits that aspiring Chaplains carry out at military facilities such as barracks, bases and naval units. ____________ The intemational events of recent years have required the use of soldiers, and consequently chaplains, for peace-keeping missions. This presence proved to be particularly useful and indispensable for both the military and the local population. A man of communion, the Chaplain actively contributes to maintaining a serene and supportive climate among the military: he shares hardships, celebrates anniversaries, animates moments of brotherhood and communion, coordinates solidarity actions for those most in need. As pastor of a community, he leads it on a path of purification and renewed impetus in faith, as a spiritual guide he knows how to find that word, discreet and eloquent, for every believer who entrusts himself to him, proposing a path of personal progression. In the meeting with the ministers of other religious confessions he expresses the supreme good of peace and the primacy of human dignity which comes before every form of belief and religion.
Publications Center for Peace Studies www.studiperlapace.it The Chaplain's attention is not only for the soldier but also for his family. He follows her from her birth with marriage training courses and accompanies her throughout her subsequent development. The family problems linked to the functionality of the military's tasks in Italy and in missions abroad are highlighted by the Pastoral Letter that the Military Ordinary published in the current year. 5.3 Career process (selection, evaluation and criteria) In particular, in harmony with the law of reorganization of the "Military Summits", the Chief of SMD - who has been given the rank of General or Admiral - assumes the Presidency of the "Summit Commission", established in each Armed Force for the evaluation of the division generals. This Commission coincides with the superior advancement commission of each Armed Force and the respective Head of SM assumes the Vice-Presidency.
The composition of the Ordinary Commission and the Superior Commission was also changed

For the above-mentioned homologous roles (operational, intermediate, logistical and technical) similar career profiles have consequently been envisaged, i.e. identical progression rates, without prejudice to the fact that the career profiles of non-homologous roles generally differ to a lesser extent than the situation pre-existing. This made it possible to remedy previous anomalous conditions, whereby the careers of Officers with the same function differed significantly depending on the Armed Force they belonged to. On the other hand, this inter-force homogenisation, while mediating positions, has also led to significant career slowdowns, in particular for access to management. This also taking into account that, as a consequence of the reorganization of the social security system for military personnel, carried out with legislative decree 30 April 1997, n. 165 - which raised the age for leaving service - it was necessary to appropriately extend the stays in certain grades of ____________ In the new system, in particular, minimum periods of time are pre-established in each grade in order to access the advancement rates, thus allowing the Administration to better plan the employment of Officers and at the same time give adequate certainties to those involved in terms of career expectations. In essence, the so-called normalized advancement system (i.e. with a fixed number of annual promotions) has been strengthened, simultaneously reiterating for the management the use of "forced redundancies" in order not to exceed the maximum number of personnel foreseen for each grade: Expectation for Reduction of Frameworks (ARQ).
This also taking into account that in the Armed Forces, career progression cannot be linked to a mere "natural" vacancy in the staff of the higher grade, but must include a pre-established number of annual promotions, deriving from a precise combination of training periods, command, employment situations, tenure in grades as well as planned recruitment.
Publications Center for Peace Studies www.studiperlapace.it With the same provision, the progression dynamics was significantly modified, previously based on evaluation rates defined as a percentage of the number of people in the role to avoid, as in the past, irregular hiring or exodus of staff from causing accelerations or slowdowns in careers.
5.3.1 Officers Legislative Decree 490/97 and subsequent amendments carried out the reorganization of the roles of Officers in a reductive key (approximately 8000 fewer units), grouping them into four types (operational, intermediate, technical and logistical), each of whom are divided into a normal role (coming from the Academies or recruited from young graduates in possession of specific professional skills, such as doctors, psychologists, engineers, etc.) and a special role (coming from the roles of Non-Commissioned Officers or Complementary Officers) .

Thus, in fact, an administrative career was created which compensates for the slowdown in the hierarchical career. Advancements in the role are all "seniority-based" with the exception of the granting of the rank of 1st Marshal and the qualification of Lieutenant which occur with the "choice" method.

5.3.4 Resignations (excluding Carabinieri) However, failure to pass the "corporal" or "corporal major" course does not, in itself, constitute grounds for dismissal from the VFB. In fact, there is the possibility of repeating the aforementioned courses, only once, and subject to the favorable opinion of the company commander and the battalion commander; and this not before the 9th month (Corporal Course) or the 23rd (Corporal Major Course). The VFBs still judged "unsuitable" will continue to serve in the short term as volunteers (if judged "unsuitable" for the corporal course) or corporal (if judged "unsuitable" for the corporal major course).
This constitutes a further step towards the materialization of that "communicating" system that allows anyone to become part of the FF.AA. and to whatever category they belong to aspire to improve their "status" position and to rise to the highest ranks.

Since 1992, in fact, the Italian social security system has been progressively aligning itself with that of other European countries based on the classic three pillars: basic public social security (mandatory and "managed" by the State), collective supplementary social security (defined through the employment contracts or concertation), a "private" pension plan (optional and linked to the possibility of individual savings).
With regard to economic treatment, with the "1996 financial law" (article 1, paragraph 63, of law 28 December 1995, n. 549) some general principles were established for military interventions abroad.

The increase is automatically attributed to personnel who leave service due to age limits, upon payment of the notified contributions for those who leave upon request.
FA Officers and Non-Commissioned Officers in permanent service are automatically registered with the "Military Funds" and are subject to the payment of social security contributions equal to 2% of 80% of their salary.
For Army Officers the percentage measure is raised to 3% to cover the provision of an additional allowance - of variable size in relation to the years of membership in the Association and the amount of the last salary received in service - in favor of staff who have left service with the right to a pension.
Finally, it is hoped that concrete recognition of the military status with respect to the dynamics of the public and private sector will also be achieved in social security treatment.

5.5 The problem of accommodation The "accommodation" problem relating to military personnel is fundamentally characterized by two aspects: an institutional one, linked to the needs of "operational functionality" of the Bodies and Commands, with a direct "reflection" on the subject of "policy of employment", and a social one, connected with the well-being of personnel, understood in the broadest sense of the quality of life of soldiers and their families. In this context, therefore, it is incontrovertible that the solution to the "accommodation problem" constitutes a primary interest of the Defense Administration with a view to being able to: • encourage the acceptance of mobility, without demotivating personnel; • guarantee, in the place of employment, the availability - certain and immediate - of adequate accom

5.6 The Military Representation The Military Representation (RM) was established with the "Principle rules on military discipline" (L. n. 382/78) which, in summary, established the fundamental legislative principles relating to the rights/duties of the citizen military and introduced Representative bodies into the military system for the first time. As a whole, the representation is an institution of the military system, made up of a set of elective collegial bodies, having the task of presenting personnel requests to the competent Military Authorities in the various sectors of interest, identified by the aforementioned establishing law. In particular, the matters it deals with are those conceming the condition, treatment, legal, economic, social security, health, cultural and moral protection of military personnel. Furthermore, the representation bodies also have jurisdiction over the following topics: • retention of jobs during compulsory military service; • entry into the world of work at the end of military service; • benefits for accidents and illnesses contracted in service and/or due to service; ____________ • Another solution could consist of paying staff a monthly allowance to cover the costs relating to the rent of accommodation found on the free market.

The common element in all these cases is the desire to abuse and marginalize those who are weaker or considered "different" for the most disparate reasons: what matters is that the pretext exists, no matter what, to unleash the aggression.
By doing so, young people will be offered the certainty of finding a serene environment, purified of archaic phenomena of abuse, and such as to encourage the choice of an interesting professional opportunity.
This is the operation that the Armed Forces are carrying out, responding positively to the requests coming from within their own sphere and from outside, giving rise to actions aimed at improving the overall image of the military world and the level of knowledge of the same that young people normally they draw from summary, and often pre-established, information.
In fact, illustrious scholars have already expressed their opinion on the subject in the past through sociological analyzes on the reasons for the deviant phenomenon, on its nature, on its dangers, on the connections with other forms of juvenile deviance also widespread in the world of school, university, sport, work.
There is, on the part of the political authorities and military leaders, the awareness that the military instrument of the future, based on entirely professional forces with a female component, will certainly be able to benefit from more adequate measures to protect personnel and enlisted personnel. in the first place.
It is not considered useful, here, to dwell on the sociological interpretation of hazing.
In fact, there have been various initiatives in this sense and among them a series of proposals deserves mention aimed both at modifying the aforementioned article 260 of the military penal code of peace and at creating the crime of hazing.
This particular attention, added to that of the leaders of the Armed Forces, forcefully affirmed the clear will of the institutions to address and resolve the problem of violence within military structures.
Much remains, however, to be done to achieve the objective that everyone, military and political authorities, have set for themselves, that is, the quality of life in the military environment, the condition of serenity of young people, attention to their problems, to the inconveniences , to the uncertainties, to the needs during military service.
But the fight against deviant behavior, as previously mentioned, has not only found the Armed Forces deployed.
In 2000, the Defense Commission of the Chamber, the first time for an institutional body of this level, addressed the problem of hazing in an in-depth and systematic manner, arranging a fact-finding investigation into the phenomenon.
This awareness, perhaps unique, was all the more important as it came to be placed in a phase in which the Armed Forces were, as they still are, engaged in a profound work of transformation which will lead them, in a short time, to a more streamlined configuration with strong professional characteristics.
87 conscripts and active duty soldiers were involved in the 54 incidents of hazing. Of these, 73 were reported to the judicial authorities,
12 punished, pursuant to art. 260 of the military penal code of peace, while 2 were punished with disciplinary sanctions, outside the cases provided for by the aforementioned art. 260.
As is known, article 260 allows the Commander, in cases of crimes punishable by a maximum of six months' imprisonment, to proceed in a disciplinary manner following communication to the military judge. ____________ It is no coincidence that in the recent past there have been more numerous initiatives undertaken in this sense by the political and military leaders.
This decisive awareness and position, beyond any other consideration, represents an important step forward in the fight against hazing: an important fight for the reaffirmation and re-establishment of legality, where violated, and to put an end to the serious damage that phenomenon caused, and still causes, to the image and credibility of the Armed Forces, which the military world has progressively built at the cost of heavy sacrifices.
As regards the most recent statistical data on the phenomenon, in the first six months of 2001 a total of fifty-four (54) incidents of hazing were recorded, which involved departments of the Army (51), Navy (2) and Air Force ( 1). Compared to 2000 (95 cases), there is a slight trend decline which, however, does not differ much from the normal trend of recent years.
Publications Center for Peace Studies www.studiperlapace.it In these conditions, the Defense's commitment to counteract intemal violence is not insignificant, a commitment that is renewed month after month with each new entry of recruits.
The Permanent Observatory on Hazing, created with this objective, after three years of activity presents itself more than ever as a reference point.
The Armed Forces, since they are closely connected with civil society - of which they represent a cross-section - are not separated from these degenerations which, on the contrary, in the military youth world, combine and integrate, favored in this by greater promiscuity. This contributes to developing that complex of deviant behaviors that determine hazing: even in 2000, in fact, a close correlation was confirmed between abusive behaviors, schooling and employment in civilian life.
Already in May 1998, the SM of Defense considered it essential to equip itself with a tool for studying "hazing" which could, through the analysis of statistical data and socio-environmental situations, provide a comprehensive and in-depth picture of the phenomenon.

____________ Publications Center for Peace Studies www.studiperlapace.it The aim of the aforementioned bill is to guarantee unconditional respect for the rights of the person, also within the Armed Forces, strengthening criminal protection against behaviors that can be traced back to "hazing".
On a technical-legal level, three distinct objectives are pursued: the introduction of new types of crime ("private violence", "ill-treatment" and "extortion") in the military penal code of peace, the provision of a specific aggravating circumstance in terms of crimes against the person and the introduction of ex officio prosecution for those crimes which represent peculiar manifestations of the phenomenon of hazing.

Basic training and qualification/specialization The complexity of the materials and means supplied to the units requires the availability of specialized personnel capable of satisfying the multiple needs of use and efficiency. Each FA, which is responsible for preparing its own forces, therefore identifies the most suitable training courses to allow its personnel - in relatively short instructional times and in any case compatible with the correct education/operational activity relationship - to acquire those "skills" necessary to be used practically and operationally in the main contexts in which the FA finds itself acting. Referring to all military personnel, basic training and qualification/specialization provides for the preparation of the individual fighter, the Commander at lower levels and the specialists.
Defense needs in the joint-force training sector The key defense needs in the joint-force training sector have been identified as: basic training and qualification/specialization, higher training, professionalisation, continuing training, European training.

The goal of creating an integrated military instrument in the European context must, therefore, be considered of high priority and, consequently, it appears necessary to make every effort to achieve it in a short time. The current political-military reality, the new strategies, the modem applied technologies constitute the parameters with which every military force will have to deal in order to achieve the necessary compatibility of use with the equal structures of other countries.
This interoperability must be sought, as a priority, in the sectors of standardization of doctrines, procedures for use and means/materials but, above all, in the field of military culture, to be built through a training of Military Cadres that is as common as possible, in a European context. at all levels.
The modem concept of "continuous training" of managers attributes to the institution the function of constantly and without interruptions both updating the knowledge/skills of its managers through every possible didactic form (short courses, intemships, handouts, conferences, involvement in training activities, distance leaming, refresher courses by correspondence, traveling professorships, e-mail, etc.), and in the creation of new professional skills that have become necessary following doctrinal or employment developments. Later in their career, the personnel in question, based on employment prospects, can participate in the activities envisaged for RN Officers following initial training. Training process for volunteers on annual leave (VFA) The first phase, relating to the 1st level of preparation, is divided into 4 modules:

The programming of the third phase training is characterized by flexibility and finalization for use, so it can be defined according to operational commitments, also providing, if necessary, for the extension or repetition of the various modules (possibly to the detriment of others) .
After obtaining the 3rd level of preparation, without particular time constraints, the individual professional qualification module is conducted, lasting 3 weeks, dedicated to professional training.

To appropriately finalize the preparation of the departments, it is necessary to conduct a series of training events which, linked together according to a consequential logic, allow the achievement of the training objectives set by the NATO STANDARDS, which then translate into operational capabilities. In this situation, in addition to individual training, different types of training activities have been defined, aimed at: Command Training It can essentially be defined as an overall training of the Cadres of a Command which Preparation for the execution of an order During this period the unit carries out the preparation activities called 1st and 2nd level, aimed at acquiring the capabilities required for the category of forces in which it is inserted (projection, reaction, presence and surveillance).
During this phase, however, a unit can be temporarily placed in a "ready to use" condition (units that are kept at a high state of readiness on a rotational basis). In this condition, the training activities developed by the departments undergo significant conditioning, related to the need to be able to be projected into operations within the times foreseen by the various states of alert. The duration of this phase varies depending on the operational commitments in progress.

The operations carried out in this context have finally influenced and made more intense the relations between the Armed Forces and the civil society of the country, sensitive to the need for "stability and security" of its region and to the Intemational activities Finally, the complex of activities described above is increased through the development of training events abroad, aimed at perfecting and consolidating the response capabilities of the Commands/units and Staff at an intemational level, privileging the Command and Control function, in order to make the instrument increasingly efficient, reliable and capable of projecting itself at a considerable distance from the Motherland and of assuming, if necessary, the direction of multinational forces.
The aspects to be taken into consideration for the checks are: • "operational" efficiency of the staff; • individual knowledge of weapons; • level of shooting training; • formal structure of the units; • self-assessment of Commands/Units; • evaluation of Commands/ Units; particular activities.
Having now confirmed the insufficiency of the purely technical-scientific study process, abandoned in the mid-90s, an integration in the legal, political-economic and historical-social fields has been undertaken. In fact, if on the one hand we note the need to possess a good mathematical-scientific basis, to consciously operate in an environment with high technological contents and use the means to their maximum potential, on the other we note the opportunity to broaden the cultural horizon to the sphere of law, politics and sociology, in order to provide greater understanding of the realities of the areas of intervention and enable personnel to operate even in situations of non-belligerence, often unplanned, in a context where even the single behavior takes on public and intemational relevance.
The review of the Officers' training process started with the adaptation of basic training programmes, an action which was also affected by the profound regulatory change in the university education sector.
Training control The organization and execution of the training involve ascertaining and evaluating, during the various phases that characterize it, the improvements obtained and the results achieved, with a view to constant application (at all levels) of the concept of "lessons leamed", for a progressive elimination of deficiencies and gaps. Therefore, a widespread and continuous control action which must have educational and only marginally fiscal purposes is fundamental. o training of units supplied with volunteers on short notice; o training of units supplied with volunteers in permanent service. o five-year courses aimed at obtaining a specialist degree, in agreement with the Universities of Pisa, Genoa, Trieste and Naples, for Officers of the Normal Role; o application courses lasting one academic year for Directly Appointed Officers and Officers of the Special Role, or course lasting 3 months for Complementary Officers. ____________ Basic training is carried out at the Naval Academy through:

The subsequent training periods are based on the foundations laid during the basic training period, aimed at completing the specific preparation for the inclusion of personnel in the different Operational Departments.

Last but not least, as regards training for combat under the surface of the sea (submarine activity and anti-submarine activity), torpedo launch activity is conducted on an annual basis for naval units and on a six-monthly basis for submarines.
The course in question has pre-eminent "value" for the FA and constitutes an indispensable and fundamental moment in the career process, characterized by strong continuity with previous personal experiences, during which the foundations are laid for the entire subsequent development of career.
Missile launch activities by naval units are carried out at the joint experimental range of Perdasdefogu, on an annual or biennial basis depending on the type of unit and the missile used.
The training period is followed by various subsequent moments, such as the "Practical Intemship" and any "Qualification Courses" on the specific equipment/systems of interest, in order to allow the Marshal to quickly integrate into new working contexts.
In the context of the topic, the innovation introduced in the basic training of new Marshals is highlighted, consisting of a two-year course at the FA Schools for the benefit of the winners of specific competitions for recruitment from the outside world, aimed at young people in possession of the diploma of upper secondary education, with the possibility of enrolling in university courses. During this first training phase we proceed to provide the basic knowledge necessary to profitably carry out the 2nd level tasks (Coordinator and/or Controller), together with the technical-professional knowledge, diversified based on the specialization to which one belongs. .
The training also takes the form of live-fire activities, where the ordinary consumption of ammunition is of the order of 100 shots/year for each embarked artillery system, an activity which also allows the accuracy of shooting against land targets to be verified (limitedly to caliber 127/54 and carried out on the Capo Teulada range), in anti-aircraft shooting (conducted on remote-controlled targets or on a hose towed by an aircraft) and anti-ship (on fast radio-controlled targets or on targets towed by another naval unit). ____________

6.1.3 Air Force 6.1.3.1 Officers The training of all personnel is divided into a basic process and an advanced process. This subdivision arises from the need to first provide those essential and indispensable elements of cultural, technical-professional and military knowledge, commensurate with the performance of the tasks/tasks that will be assigned in the initial years of the career; subsequently the advanced process provides for the improvement and specialization of the military personnel called upon to carry out tasks of greater responsibility and growing importance which cannot be separated from more rigorous preparation. • ____________ to the conduct of the maneuver of naval units in favor of young officers at the Naval Academy of Livomo;
Training is also integrated through the use of special training simulators:
Publications Center for Peace Studies www.studiperlapace.it • As regards the ranges and training areas, these are sufficient, except for those connected to training in complex amphibious activity, for which only the Capo Teulada range, owned by the Army, is available, which is unable to satisfy the needs of the Armed Forces in their entirety.
The training methodologies used in the aforementioned centers are similar to those in use at the Basic Operational Sea Training and the Joint Marittime Course of the British Navy and the Mine warfare Operational Sea Training developed by the Belgian and Dutch Navies, centers considered leaders within NATO in the specific sectors and also used by the Italian Navy for appropriate evaluation and comparison activities.
The Taranto MM Training Center carries out evaluations and checks on the training of units through TIRNAV (Naval Intemships) and CLA (Training Level Control); TIRNAVs are carried out at the end of long-term maintenance stops, while CLAs represent an additional training phase when, following a check, deficiencies are found in one or more sectors. The specialized mine countermeasures units carry out checks called CEB (war efficiency control), carried out by the Dredging Forces Command, to evaluate the combat readiness of the aforementioned units according to a preestablished test programme.
The Armed Force also uses simulators owned by the Army for anti-tank combat training by amphibious force personnel, according to times and methods agreed between the two Armed Forces.
Activities to control the level of training/efficiency are implemented through the chain of command of the Armed Forces; each commander has the responsibility to evaluate the efficiency of his unit or department, on the basis of appropriate directives issued by the higher commands.
