Role of seamen The AM takes care of the training of seafarers through a complex and articulated selective/educational and training structure, which includes academic courses on the ground, on the simulator and in flight. Furthermore, initial or basic courses via computer are used more and more often. The management of the structure is mainly entrusted to the General Command of the Schools. The selection and training programs are implemented through national and foreign structures, the latter managed through specific MoUs. The periodic review of the programs allows the contents to be kept updated in light of new needs. The challenge for the future is to adapt the programs in question to the needs of the new flight lines being acquired (EFA, C130J, B767, F16, C 27). Among the long-term programs (post 2010), which could provide the greatest qualitative retum for the country, the project of a European flight school on our territory stands out.
The entire process has as its final objective that of preparing both culturally and professionally the Officer to assume (possibly also at a managerial level given the possibility of holding the rank of Colonel) staff positions within the High Command and, marginally, in the Air Force General Staff.
In this regard, the personnel of the corps follows specific degree courses already covered by the university system which are integrated with technical-professional subjects considered indispensable.
Training for normal roles involves obtaining a degree and a specialist degree from the Air Force Academy (for the role of navigators and the Armed Forces, the possibility of also involving the Florentine university is envisaged).
Subsequently, there are two other significant training periods in the ranks of Captain and Lieutenant Colonel which are carried out at the Air War School of Florence lasting 3 and 2 months respectively. prepare attendees to carry out the technical-professional functions and duties envisaged by the new "status", so that they are able to carry out specific tasks pertaining to the Army and the Role they belong to, as well as knowing how to apply previous knowledge in the military field. o is aimed at providing the necessary knowledge to carry out the assigned tasks.

Furthermore, the Air Force attaches great importance to the training carried out by its Operations Departments. The training process to which the flight personnel is subjected is a long and onerous process which begins with the assignment to the operational conversion group, relating to the line of employment. After the transition to the new machine, a second phase of conversion to the weapon system is entered. Upon completion, the qualification of limited Combat Ready is achieved. Full Combat Readiness is achieved once the sailor demonstrates the ability to use the aircraft in all tactical events specific to the specialty to which he belongs. This milestone allows participation in any real operations. In fact, to carry out the normal procedures of an advanced training phase, systems are needed that reproduce real performances with great fidelity. Consequently, costs increase and an accurate evaluation of the cost/effectiveness ratio of the simulators to be acquired with respect to carrying out training operations with scheduled aircraft is necessary (for example, some modem flight simulators allow the qualification on the machine to " zero hours" by the member in training, i.e. without carrying out any dedicated flight on the real airplane) even if the costs are however so high (a simulator of this kind can cost as much as two new aircraft), as to advise against purchasing the simulator if you don't have a large number of crews to train.
In fact, although part of the procedures and elementary operations can be carried out on systems comparable with commercial personal computers with a very high cost/effectiveness ratio, a different situation occurs with regards to advanced training.

6.1.4.4 Training activity for departments assigned to military tasks The Carabinieri departments assigned to military tasks must be able to perform various functions simultaneously, expressing a high degree of flexibility and therefore force devices to be calibrated based on the type and phase of the mission, the level of military and social conflict and the different contingent situations of the OSP (Public Order and Security), both abroad and at home. • Among the departments responsible for the specialized training of Army personnel, the following should also be mentioned: through the presentation of real cases, as well as the preparation of simulations. This last training function was entrusted to qualified Company and Station commanders, selected nationally on the basis of personal qualities and previous professional experience in order to guarantee a direct link with the operational reality. The activity, which completes the experiences acquired during the practical-applicative intemships carried out at various levels, also made it possible to create a team of experts, essentially incorporating the figure of the commander-teacher, employable for limited periods of time and , with good reason, in the training sector. • • ____________ • Publications Center for Peace Studies www.studiperlapace.it Finally, great importance is given to the teaching of applied information technology which has been included in all training and refresher courses for the Army personnel: since 2001, Officers, Marshals and Carabinieri who leave the training courses obtain the "European IT licence" and the qualification to consult and update police files.
Maximum impetus is given to the study of the English language and the so-called "rare" languages to satisfy the pressing operational needs imposed by immigration and the globalization of the terrorist threat and organized crime. • Furthermore, it is worth mentioning the numerous activities carried out, also in collaboration with university structures and extemal institutes operating in the sector, aimed at the qualification of personnel from a linguistic point of view, to meet the needs arising from the intensification of relations with the Armed Forces and of foreign police forces and the development of the intemational dimension of the force, connected to participation in peacekeeping missions. As evidence of the extent of the resources devoted to the specific sector, it should be highlighted that, in the last two years, approximately 5,000 soldiers of the Army, of all ranks, have been interested in attending language courses or have participated in assessments to verify their level of knowledge possessed.

7.2 STAFF
7.1 CIVIL PERSONNEL The first redetermination of the workforce in the above-mentioned reduction perspective was carried out with the Prime Ministerial Decree of 26 June 1998 which established the total workforce at 45,740 units, for which as of 1
June 2001 there were 40,304 employees with a shortage of 2,696 compared to the staff expected when fully operational (43,000 units).
7.2 Staffing The staffing of civilian personnel was established with Legislative Decree no. 265/1997 in 43,000 units, to be reached at the conclusion of the restructuring process and "in any case within eight years of the entry into force of the same legislative decree" (August 2005). ____________ However, the aforementioned shortcomings could also be satisfied through the activation of the training courses envisaged by the National Collective Labor Agreement (CCNL), which would allow, with the professional advancement of employees, a more rapid fulfillment of personnel needs, felt above all in higher qualifications.
Civilian personnel, in relation to the significant reduction of the military component, is destined to take on increasingly greater importance over time at all organizational levels, both central and peripheral.
It is clear that in order to completely make up for the staff shortages through extemal recruitment, the related costs and the financial resources needed to deal with them cannot be ignored.
The staff retraining procedures, still underway, will produce an initial effect of making up for the deficiencies especially in positions C2 and B3, but will have as a consequence a corresponding increase in the deficiencies in area C, as regards the economic position C1. This provision may be overcome, in whole or in part, either with extemal recruitment, the reactivation of which took place last year following the expected authorizations from the Presidency of the Council of Ministers, or with the re-employment of any redundancies among the staff military, in implementation of law n.331/2000, establishing the professional military service.
The employees in question are managed by the General Directorate for Civilian Personnel established in 1997 with the simultaneous suppression of the pre-existing General Directorates of Employees and Workers.
The functions of the civil component are mainly administrative and partly technical, all aimed at supporting the military instrument as a whole.

7.1 Civilian personnel The Defense structure includes a substantial and valid civilian component. Nonetheless, organizational models require such performance. Hence the need to resort to extemal companies that guarantee such services.
Some services that must necessarily be ensured (in particular cleaning services, canteens, etc.) for their completion require tasks that are no longer attractive to Defense employees in the current socio-cultural context.
In this context, the result is a reduction in lower level staff (positions A1 and B1), with exclusively manual and executive tasks.
7.4 Use of extemal structures (Outsourcing) This provision, without prejudice to the spending constraints relating to civilian personnel, will have to take into account the new employment positions emerging from the ongoing restructuring which show a significant need, in medium-high qualifications, deriving on the one hand from the need to acquire new professional skills especially in the technical and IT sector, on the other hand by the expected progressive replacement of military personnel with civilian personnel for the performance of logistical and administrative support functions.
On the other hand, staff mobility, if contained within acceptable terms, does not generally encounter resistance. If, on the contrary, one wanted to use it as a general tool to meet necessary needs, one would encounter insurmountable obstacles, both due to the physiological reaction of the staff who see consolidated family, housing, relational and income situations compromised, and due to the current impossibility, on the part of the Administration, to be able to reduce the inconveniences resulting from a new location into acceptable areas.
In the situation described above, with respect to the gradual process of approaching the workforce of 43,000 units, the staffing levels referred to in the aforementioned Prime Ministerial Decree of 26 June 1998 will be redetermined.
For extemal recruitment, and within the foreseen and possible limits, the coverage of those positions for which filling from within is not practicable could be reserved.
In fact, faced with oversized areas, in terms of the presence of civilian personnel, there are situations of critical undersizing.
In this context, it will be necessary to verify the existence of adequate professional figures to manage and guarantee the implementation of the structure envisaged by art.5, paragraph 1, of law 16 November 2000, n.331, which will have to provide for the employment of military personnel dismissed volunteers.
Therefore, in these cases, it is not so much the containment of costs that is highlighted, but the unavoidable need to pursue and achieve institutional goals.
7.3.2 An aspect that deserves reflection and that will have to be addressed to reduce, within physiological limits, the obstacles that stand in the way of greater use of civilian personnel within the new professional structure of the Armed Forces is certainly to be identified in the non-optimal distribution of the same in the territory. ____________

Therefore, outsourcing opportunities must be carefully evaluated, either due to financial problems or due to the objective impossibility of having the necessary human resources.

Trade union participation, beyond the formulas which only abstractly seem to strengthen its role, essentially has dialogue and discussion as its essence. It is in the dialectics of the parties, in the strength of the motivations and in the need to achieve certain goals that it is necessary to find the resulting solutions that best balance the various interests at stake.
Other reflections will be made on the topic and this is why the Govemment has asked for a delegation from Parliament to be able to update an initial intuition, which must be verified and correlated with changing situations and events that push in directions that may even be different from those in initially hypothesized.
Up until now there has probably been a lack of a personnel policy which, in addition to management, which in any case should not be underestimated, due to the virtuous effects it can produce, if taken care of in terms of timeliness and effectiveness of administrative action, prefigures a development of human resources.
But beyond the numbers, the figures, the recruitment and advancement systems, an irreplaceable and decisive element can only be identified in the subjective response that everyone is called to give in their work activity.
The appreciated action carried out by the national and local trade union organizations has made it possible to achieve, albeit sometimes amidst physiological difficulties and divergent points of view, positive results for the start of the reform process whose state of progress is not yet definitive drawing.

7.5 Civilian personnel: a new concept. Perspectives Various elements can have an impact on enabling a Defense performance adequate to intemal and extemal expectations.
It cannot be ruled out, without prejudice to the activity to be carried out by the Administration, that, also for this need, the opportunity of outsourcing certain professional contributions that contribute to the optimal achievement of the pursued objectives may be assessed.
All of this can find a valid aid, on the one hand by rediscovering the awareness of belonging to an Administration which has never before been called upon, at a national and intemational level, to express a serene determination in acting, on the other by assuming, to maximum levels of responsibility, coherent behavior that knows how to interpret values to be strengthened and transmitted to all collaborators, in a renewed spirit of common action involving the entire working community. ____________ Here then is the need on a personal level to set daily objectives which in temporal sequence will be able to achieve the primary objectives of the Administration; even long-term objectives require the achievement of short-and medium-term objectives.
Obviously everyone without distinction, at every level, is required to deal with the dynamics of the organization so that the same, in a constant evolution, maintains high profiles of the institutional image.
Publications Center for Peace Studies www.studiperlapace.it The joumey will be easy to the extent that it is understood that we proceed side by side in the awareness of a path whose destination is common.
Particular attention must be paid above all to the identification of the so-called "key resources", understood as units that are potentially called upon to play a decisive role in making the Defense function competitive.
There is no doubt that the sum of the performances thus rendered optimizes the system.
Therefore, there is no prejudice in addressing together with the Union problems which affect the future of the Administration and whose repercussions also affect the staff.
The valorization of the individual, when inserted in a working context, constitutes a multiplier of performances, both in qualitative and quantitative terms.

MILITARY JUSTICE
PART VIII The CMM is, in fact, competent to decide on every state provision conceming military magistrates and on every other matter delegated to it by law.
In order to guarantee the same independence recognized to ordinary judges, the Council has, as already mentioned, the same powers as the CSM, including those conceming disciplinary proceedings against military magistrates. ____________
8.2 Council of the Military Magistracy With law 30.12.1988, n.561, the CMM was established, a self-goveming body, subsequently regulated with Presidential Decree of 24.3.1989, n.158.
Publications Center for Peace Studies www.studiperlapace.it In each military court, a 1st degree judicial body, an office of the judge for preliminary investigations and one of the judge for the preliminary hearing is established, the organization of which has no particularities compared to the corresponding offices existing in the ordinary courts.
8.3 Military judicial organization The military judicial offices are divided into prosecuting bodies (Military General Prosecutor's Office at the Supreme Court of Cassation; General Prosecutor's Office at the Military Court of Appeal and 9 Military Prosecutor's Offices at the Military Tribunals) and judging bodies (Military Court of Appeal, 9 Military Courts and Military Surveillance Court).
Law no. 180 of 7 May 1981 made a series of changes to the military judicial system.
The art. 103 of the Constitution attributes, in peacetime, to military courts the exercise of jurisdiction for military crimes committed by members of the Armed Forces.
The current Council - which is the fourth by the founding law - took office on 31 July 2001.
8.1 General information The Minister of Defense has, with respect to the military magistrates and the Council of the Military Judiciary, CMM, the same powers that belong to the Minister of Justice with respect to the Superior Council of the Magistracy (CSM), and the ordinary magistrates.
163


164 Furthermore, it is necessary to keep in mind the workload that resulted from the new code of criminal procedure which introduced (1989) the so-called "accusatory" rite in which the evidence is formed with the cross-examination of the parties, as well as the new institution of the Judge's Offices for preliminary investigations (gip) and of the judge for the preliminary hearing (gup).
This means that if numerically the workload may appear to have decreased, in reality these types of "character" crimes, due to the difficulties of investigations and hearings, determine a much higher work commitment than the previous one.
The judging panel of the Military Court of Appeal is made up of the president of the Court itself or, in case of impediment, by a military magistrate of Cassation or Appeal, acting as president; by two military appellate magistrates, with judge functions; by two soldiers of one of the FF.AA. or of the Guardia di Finanza, of a rank equal to that of the accused and, in any case, not lower than lieutenant colonel, drawn by lot, with the functions of judge.
In fact, if on the one hand it is true that statistically there has been a decrease in the typical crimes committed by enlisted personnel, so-called "absence" crimes (failure to be called up, desertion, etc.), it is equally true that the so-called "absence" crimes have increased. "character", especially some types of crimes such as property crimes against the PA.
The Military Court of Appeal also devolves the powers that art.45 of the military judicial system (RD 9 September 1941, n.1022) attributed to the Supreme Military Court in special composition (military rehabilitation; reinstatement in rank, etc.).
This framework must not, however, lead us to believe - as supported by the Council itself in a recent resolution - that the work and needs of military judicial offices, with the reduction of conscript personnel, are necessarily destined to decrease.
The second level body is the Military Court of Appeal, established with art.3 of law 7.5.1981, n.180, which judges on the appeal lodged against the measures issued by the military courts.
Pursuant to law 180/81, military courts judge with the intervention of the president of the military court, who presides over it or, in case of impediment, of an appellate military magistrate, acting as president; of a military tribunal or appellate magistrate, with the functions of a judge; of a soldier of one of the FF.AA. or of the Guardia di Finanza, of a rank equal to that of the accused and in any case not lower than the rank of officer, chosen by lot, with the functions of judge.
The data relating to the military judicial appellate offices, the surveillance ones and the military general prosecutor's office at the Supreme Court of Cassation highlight how judicial activity is numerically limited and, in any case, tends to decrease. ____________ The first level prosecuting offices, however, in the same reference year, carried out criminal proceedings in approximately
4300 cases, compared to almost 9000 in 1997 (minus 50%).
8.4 Judicial activity: statistical data During the year 2000 - as emerges from the results of a fact-finding investigation promoted by the Military Judiciary Council - the military judicial offices of first instance pronounced 4010 trial sentences (of which 870 plea bargaining), at which must be added to the 1500 sentences rendered by the judges for the preliminary hearing - these figures are almost constant compared to those of recent years.
Publications Center for Peace Studies www.studiperlapace.it In fact, compared to a staff of 103 military magistrates, there are currently 83 presences to which must be added another 13 recently appointed units who are completing the hearing period, upon completion of which they will be placed in the relevant functions.
The aforementioned Court is made up of all the military supervisory magistrates and experts appointed by the CMM, within the categories indicated by art.80, IV paragraph, of law 26.7.1975, n.354 (professionals expert in psychology, service social, pedagogy and psychiatry), as well as among teachers of criminal sciences.

8.9 The Military Penitentiary Organization (OPM – Executive Bodies) At the moment, however, despite the absence of the specific legislative vehicle, the needs reported over the years by the various military prosecutors have sometimes been satisfied by resorting to the assignment, on an actual level only, of the requested personnel belonging to the Carabinieri.
Currently, in fact, due to the promiscuity of military and civilian prisoners, custodial treatments and judicial skills, military supervisory personnel must in fact carry out typical functions of a "maximum security" IPC according to laws and regulations which at the moment are heterogeneous between them, with the assumption by the same of responsibilities typical of civil organisation.
This reality, with increasingly frequent "assignments" of "particular detainees" subjected to typical "maximum security" IPC surveillance, has accentuated the "deviation of employment" of an organization normally dedicated to tasks of an exclusively military nature.
On this point it seems appropriate that an organic discipline be prepared taking into account the peculiar military condition and ensuring the harmonization of the tasks and responsibilities of the PGM of the Corps Commanders with the attributions proposed for the Sections whose establishment can be analyzed in the context of a broader reform of the military criminal justice system.

The term "war" raises scenarios in the collective imagination that we would like to exorcise with the abolition of the word from common language. But the time has come to rationalize the matter and bring the legal situation into line with the factual one.
The suspension always sanctioned by the CPMG Legislator with respect to military forces employed abroad probably has its rationale in factors of a more psychological than legal nature.
It is necessary not only to project ourselves into the medium-long term and try to anticipate the times, but also to revisit options which, due to their shared opportunity, could be introduced, if innovative in the current system, into the legal world.
The profiles that could be addressed concem both the competence of the military judiciary and, in an inseparable connection, the legal system.
The only fixed point remains the jurisdiction of the Military Court of Rome regarding all military crimes committed abroad by members of the deployed contingents.
In this context, so far we have tried to make up for it with intemal instructions and with the so-called "rules of engagement", the violation of which may only be relevant on a disciplinary level. However, given the vastness of the matter, they cannot be considered as solving the problem.
If on the one hand the marked autonomy of this system constitutes the founding reason, on the other hand such a choice may not be entirely coherent and should at least be adequately investigated in order to be confirmed.
Furthermore, a further effect of the choice made by the Legislator is to be found in the inapplicability in military operations abroad of the rules of war law, intemational humanitarian law and those provisions aimed at allowing better protection of our soldiers.
8.10 Considerations The reform plan relating to the Defense Administration, not yet completed, has not yet affected the military judicial administration. The area of military justice has remained almost unchanged over time.
At the same time as the aforementioned provision, the Govemment, given the significant implications of a substantive and procedural nature, also approved a bill containing amendments to the military criminal code of war itself, not limited to the "Enduring-Freedom" operation alone.
However, it was necessary to establish the non-application of the provisions on military war procedure and those conceming the relevant judicial system to this operation. ____________ The solution adopted also results in the impossibility of prosecuting the so-called "war crimes" whose cases are contained in the articles. from 165 to 230 cpmg which at the time was very early on this point compared to the intemational standards in force, but which over time would have required an update in light of the conventions subsequently ratified on the subject by Italy.
If this is the reference rule on the matter, it must be said that up to now the Legislator, on the occasion of the numerous missions abroad that our Armed Forces. were called upon to carry out, he chose to apply only the military penal code of peace.
Publications Center for Peace Studies www.studiperlapace.it This will entail particular criminal protection inherent to the military condition and interest and will allow the application of some rules against the laws and customs of war, i.e. humanitarian law.
Well, it is for these significant and unavoidable purposes that the Govemment has taken charge of the problem and with a decree-law, given the extraordinary need and urgency to issue provisions aimed at regulating Italian participation in the multinational operation called "Enduring-Freedom" against terrorism, has applied the military criminal code of war to the personnel employed.
The provision, establishing the application of the military criminal law of war to all Corps - land, naval and air crews - when they are sent abroad for military actions, allows both the exercise of criminal action against all those typical codified crimes in this law possibly committed by Italian soldiers, and the prosecutability of specific crimes committed against the latter.
For the crews of military ships or military aircraft, subjection to the military criminal law of war begins from the moment in which the destination of the shipment is communicated to them".
It is in the interest of the FF.AA. and operators have an updated reference framework that is certain and compatible with constitutional principles, with ratified intemational conventions and with emerging cases dictated by operational experience. The results can only be entirely positive on a substantive and procedural level.
8.11 Application of the military penal code of peace (and not of war) during military missions abroad – Problematic aspects Art. 9 cpmg, dating back like the peace law to 1941, prescribes that: "Expeditionary forces abroad for military operations are subject to the military criminal law of war, even in peacetime, from the moment in which the crossing the borders of the State and, in the case of an overseas shipment, from the moment the expeditionary force begins to embark.

9.1 Logistics - General "Logistics" has always represented the essential element for the life and activity of any complex organization and, even more so, for that of Defence. It has the fundamental purpose of placing operational forces in a position to conduct and complete the missions entrusted to them. As is known, the strategic security framework in Europe and around the world has changed considerably in recent times. This change has led most countries, particularly those belonging to NATO, to redefine their role and tasks. In this regard, the Atlantic Alliance has developed a new strategic concept based on the concept of "Collective Defense and Security". Consistent with this renewed approach, NATO has already started a process of intemal structural adaptation of its military organization to make it more responsive to new scenarios, to give due importance, within the Alliance, to the European Security and Defense Identity (ESDI ) and for the acquisition of better operational capabilities, to respond to the new tasks and missions related to crisis management and the risks of proliferation of weapons of destruction of In particular, for the Navy there is a need to have a support tool that allows it to continuously maintain, at the usual levels of operational readiness, various Force complexes (from the single unit, to air-naval power projection groups, mine clearance, of amphibious intervention, of escorts and protection, of advanced covert surveillance, etc.) deployed anywhere in intemational waters with very short notice, very high technical-logistical autonomy and without the need for theater bases.

Commissariat 9.6.3.3 The Mobile Support Department It is therefore necessary to seek the prerequisites for a common direction on the matter which, while respecting the peculiar employment philosophy of each Armed Force, allows efforts to converge for the optimal achievement of the objectives. On this basis, it is therefore important that the military instrument has "finalized logistics", in other words aimed at supporting the projection of forces outside the area, a logistics which, through the synergistic use of information, materials, healthcare and transport, makes the mobility and supply of operational units possible in a timely, operationally optimal manner and with a favorable cost-effectiveness ratio. ____________ It will therefore be necessary to substantiate the fundamental principles of redevelopment of the military instrument, such as: timeliness, deployment, integrability, interoperability and sustainability. All this inevitably presupposes a radical change in those logistical concepts that have govemed the sector up to now, based on territoriality and on a certain rigidity and burdensomeness due to the traditional concept of material storage with all the related organizational and technical-administrative. In this context, it has therefore become imperative to radically review the organizational framework of logistics, with particular attention to Logistics Planning, which must be conducted with a joint vision of the inter-forces, seeking every possible integration between the Armed Forces aimed at optimizing the available resources.
It follows that, at a national level, the choices and structural adaptations must be compatible and coherent with the European and Atlantic ones within a framework of full integration and interoperability with the Armed Forces of the allied countries.

That is, the Italian Armed Forces ultimately lack the depth to sustain over time even limited efforts or efforts in differentiated theaters unless there is a suitable increase in the sector of the budget dedicated to materials.

Consequently, an active, efficient and long-term military participation, which caused a strong wear and tear of "materials". In this regard, the most significant comparison can only be translated into terms of comparison between the operational capabilities provided in relation to the resources - always limited - invested and which are invested to achieve them. The Defense, in fact, abandoning the old policy which encouraged the purchase of materials produced domestically, resorting to acquisition abroad only in case of necessity and absolute economic convenience, has long directed its action towards constant intemational market research. With regard to the procurement of materials relating to "Logistics", i.e. materials/services relating to the technical-logistical support of operational units, the Defense intends to pursue a procurement policy based on the following criteria: • accentuated use of out-sourcing on a of convenience-necessity; • transfer to the Technical-Operational Area of all maintenance activities - including supply - in compliance with the principle that, as these are logistical activities dedicated to the support of operational units, these must necessarily be the responsibility of the Armed Forces;

Furthermore, the ever-increasing amount of information to be transmitted, together with the need for global coverage, presupposes the opportunity to complement typically military systems with dual and/or commercial systems, capable of ensuring the satisfaction of needs that are not strictly operational or cover particular geographical areas outside the footprint of military satellites.
The second need that must be satisfied by future communications systems will therefore be that of making a large amount of information available to the operational user in times close to reality and with a man/machine interface that is as user friendly as possible.
SICRAL and RNI pertain to the transport of information: the first with a prevalent strategic connotation (out-of-area operations, mobile users), the second with a prevalent infrastructural connotation (connections between the Command centers located on the national territory). The two systems are perfectly integrated with each other, to the point that one can be considered an extension of the other, thus achieving interoperability at joint force level. Furthermore, the two systems can accept/provide services from/to similar systems of allied countries, thus responding to the principle of multinational interoperability.
All the aforementioned systems integrate joint forces needs at different levels and within the limits of the particular operational needs for which they were conceived. In particular:

It is in fact clear that since 7 February 2001 the availability of satellite communications has become an indispensable requirement for Defense, a prerequisite for the correct exercise of the Command and Control of national forces. This means that study activities aimed at guaranteeing the continuity of the service even after 2011 have already been started. Obviously the operational experience of use in the field, the emerging communications needs and the foreseeable developments of technology in the next decade will constitute an element fundamental to such studies.

They are associated with other positive advantages, such as the creation of integrated logistical support shared with other nations and the retum of know-how for the Italian defense industry.
RUPA is the most important intersectoral project pursued by AIPA for the creation of a telematic network in which all public administrations are integrated with each other through the services offered (electronic mail, file transfer, access to news, virtual terminal and INTERNET ). This network requires that each Administration must "approach" it with its own "domain", understood as the set of hardware resources (computers, networks, etc.) and software (applications and data) that belong to the same organization.
The need to have a telematic network that would allow the connection between the computers and the networks of the Defense Administration regardless of their geographical location and the operating system of the various IT systems, was already strongly felt by the Armed Forces. It became unavoidable when the problem of connecting the Defense Administration to the Unitary Network of Public Administration (RUPA) had to be addressed in compliance with the provisions of the law on the matter. the containment of high research and development costs, currently sustainable only through their distribution between several countries; maintaining maximum interoperability with the systems of the allied armies. ____________

Furthermore, it is worth underlining that the completion of the programs currently included in the investment planning for the period 2002-2010 does not allow obtaining a result that is completely in line with the real needs of the Armed Forces. In fact, to adapt the combat capabilities of the IE to European and DCI standards, it would be necessary to implement a series of programs that have not yet been launched and for which additional resources would be needed for an amount equal to approximately 3,000 billion. Among these , in particular, include the provision of total night vision capability to the entire range of forces (currently this is only foreseen for a portion of personnel), the acquisition of 3rd generation anti-tank systems in a shorter timeframe ( 5 - 7 years instead of the current 13), the replacement of the materials of two Heavy Field Artillery Regiments (currently equipped with FH-70s), the acquisition of artillery systems for Deep Operations (ATACMS) and the reduction of time for the updating of the Engineers' materials, necessary for carrying out the multiple tasks carried out by this type of force in the context of peace support operations. • If we take 2010 as a reference, we realize that in a decade the Fleet will find itself in a condition of critical obsolescence: apart from the age of the most recent Units (Garibaldi 25 years, De La Penne and Mimbelli 20 years), the age of the frigates in line will fluctuate between 25 and 30 years. ____________

9.3 Policy of intemational cooperation in the field of the transfer of materials As previously mentioned, another aspect that increasingly characterizes the Italian Armed Forces is that of multinationality. This is an increasingly evident peculiarity of the intemational scenario, where on the one hand the general decrease in defense resources and on the other the growing spirit of cooperation even between countries that were once adversaries leads them to pool their forces. As is known, the Italian Armed Forces are undergoing a strong downsizing, moving from a mixed force level of 250 thousand units towards a professional model of 190 thousand units by December 2004. This downsizing, at the same time as a more elaborate political push to technological renewal initiated by the Defense has made a large quantity of means and materials available, as they were surplus to requirements.
Consistent with this renewed approach, the availability of materials and means (even if largely technologically outdated) and their transfer have constituted and constitute a valid and concrete instrument of foreign policy, in particular towards those member countries of the Partnership for Peace ( PfP).
• identify an armed force to which to assign joint responsibility for the development of operational requirements for equipment and materials with similar characteristics, even if in use by different armed forces; • proceed with collective acquisitions; ____________ In this context, an intense activity has developed, substantiated by the tendency to establish "Agreements" regarding cooperation with various Nations, giving away the exuberant material free of charge as shown in the following table:
The new teleintercom systems for the stations are already operational which allow you to immediately contact the department staff involved in carrying out extemal services, equipped with a special mobile phone, or, if the mobile device is tumed off or unreachable, to establish video contact directly with the Operations Center of the Carabinieri Company responsible for the emergency telephone district (112). will allow for careful planning of services and the use of personnel, encouraging the development of operational statistics and the analysis of criminal events, as well as the management of soldiers with regards to weekly rest periods, leaves of absence and overtime , etc.).
9.4 Operation Policy Any choice made on the technological and operational level of the use of forces has a direct and immediate impact on the operation sector, understood as the activities necessary to preserve, guarantee, ascertain and restore efficiency, suitability to the use of materials and the right degree of reliability, effectiveness and safety based on the original operational requirement. As is known, this activity requires increasing costs as they are linked to the technology of the increasingly sophisticated systems themselves. This requires pursuing a policy oriented, already in the research phase, towards systems capable of containing the operational needs, within acceptable limits and in any case preestablished in the phase of definition of the military technical-operational requirements: in other words it is necessary to consider from the first phases of studying the "Life Cycle Cost" (LCC) of each system, by carrying out accurate cost analyzes (LCA).
Technological modemization naturally also involves the research and acquisition of materials and equipment that better respond to the needs of personnel in different operational situations: from new equipment for public order to sophisticated equipment for scientific investigations.
For a more profitable and flexible use of connections, new digital telephone exchanges are being installed, which will reduce the number of current analogue switchboards with a significant recovery of units and an improvement in the quality of services.
In this context, there are many initiatives that can be adopted, each of which requires dedicated in-depth analysis, such as: • directing the planned interventions towards the civil industry; • identify inter-force maintenance centers of excellence, at existing military structures, where all similar equipment and weapon systems, even if from different armed forces, can be brought together for the most complex maintenance activities;
MAIN TRANSFERS MADE FREE OF CHARGE TO THIRD COUNTRIES


183 Unit maintenance activities in operations In operations the Units must preserve their mobility, especially in high intensity operations and in a non-permissive environment.
Therefore, the types of interventions that users can carry out, contrary to what happens on site, tend downwards, transferring the most expensive interventions to the GSA.
Newly introduced materials are usually accompanied by a warranty assistance service for the first years of life.
9.4.1 Land segment The Army, in relation to the operational environment in which it operates, has configured logistics in two areas.
The maintenance activities of the Units in garrison In the garrison the maintenance activity varies depending on the family to which the material belongs (commercial vehicles, tactical vehicles, combat vehicles, weapon systems, etc.) and depending on the capabilities needed for repair ; it can be carried out at the user of the material, at specialized FA workshops, at specialized civil companies and finally at the companies manufacturing the material. Usually users are responsible for scheduled maintenance and checks; furthermore, in relation to the available resources (specialised personnel, spare parts and equipment) they can also carry out repairs of a certain complexity.
Piling of supplies to combat units must be avoided, in order not to limit their mobility. of arms.
• standardize, at joint level, the criteria for defining the life cycle of materials and systems Supplies to units in operations In operations the units are supplied by a department called the Adherence Support Group (GSA). This department, of variable composition, is calibrated for the specific mission. In low-conflict operations it is possible to outsource some services in order to recover specialized logistics structures. ____________ Supplies to the garrisoned Units The supply of the garrisoned Units is ensured from the Support Area. Through careful planning of the users' needs, cyclically or ad hoc, it brings the requested materials to the permanent location. To carry out this task, the Support Area uses warehouses/depots located throughout the national territory, using a transport system dedicated to this task.
The analysis will be divided in relation to the operational environment in which they operate: in garrison and in operations.

9.4.2 Naval segment In the Navy, the tasks relating to logistics functions are institutionally divided between the MM Commissariat and Administrative Coordination Inspectorate (MARISPECOMMI) for the sectors of MEMBERSHIP AREA: responsible for logistical support of combat units in operations. Its Command, Management and Executive bodies are all capable of operating without fixed infrastructures and can be projected outside the national territory. Furthermore, they are able to operate in non-permissive environments with a high level of conflict;
SUPPORT AREA: responsible for direct support to all FA units/bodies when they are in garrison. Guarantees the procurement and availability of the materials necessary for the Membership Area for operations. It ensures, through the management of FA stocks, the continuous availability of resources. Its Command, Management and Executive bodies cannot be projected and can only operate in fixed installations.
Regardless of the working hypotheses illustrated above, the organizational structures that currently characterize the sector are reported below.
Since the mid-eighties, the FA has moved from a logistical organization "by subject" to one "by function". In summary, the operators in the sector, before 1980, acted and were divided according to the type of material on which they operated (light weapons, artillery, engineering, police station, etc.), following the restructuring, the reference of the organizational architecture is in relation to the function that operators perform regardless of the type of material. Here today we find specialized personnel and entire operational units that are interested in supplies, others who are dedicated to maintenance and still others responsible for transport etc..

9.4.2.5 Vehicles The MM vehicles are the vehicles used for "technical, operational, training and logistical services" destined for the Commands/Bodies of the technical-operational area of the Navy. The vehicle acquisition (executed by the competent DG) and maintenance program is identified annually on the basis of the funds made available in the budget and in relation to the needs for replacing old vehicles and maintenance of the vehicle fleet.

That is, the spare parts must be taken over by the Administration and only subsequently passed back to the company that carries out the work on the aircraft.
The daily functioning of the Italian Air Force with particular regard to aircraft efficiency issues is based, among other elements, on the management system of materials relating to flight activities. These include both aircraft spare parts and all ground equipment necessary for their support and maintenance. The management of the aforementioned materials is carried out following the classic logistical functions of acquisition, conservation, distribution and disposal.
For the entire operational life of the aircraft there is also the need to organize the repair of materials rendered inefficient due to unexpected breakdowns or due to scheduled overhaul of complex devices. These interventions can be carried out at Armed Forces entities or at extemal industry.
9.4.3.1 Bureaucratic procedures Furthermore, this reality also effectively prevents relief formulas for the Administration in managing efficiency.
Experience shows that this system presents evident characteristics of inadequacy and inefficiency, often compensated by the good will and quality of the staff involved. assigned tasks, imposes decisive action in the direction of the strong rationalization of all sectors and the resulting need to increase the level of "outsourcing" of certain logistical functions (in the sectors, for example, of the technical-maintenance support of systems supplied on the levels does not directly affect operations) where it can be potentially convenient in terms of cost/benefit of the utility provided.
The ongoing contraction of the FA's organic consistency, in the face of the need to continue to honor the further step must be carefully documented and approved. Particularly complex is the so-called load transfer (i.e. the physical/accounting possession of the material) between the Armed Forces and any repair industry, as well as the intemal passage of the repaired material subjected to acceptance and conformity procedures envisaged by the repair contract, always based on paper documentation. ____________ The reality described above, in its concrete daily unfolding, leads to an inevitable contradiction between the urgencies dictated by flight activity and the slavish paper bureaucracy required by every single movement of parts. Within the framework of the policy of optimizing resources through a targeted recovery of personnel from sectors that are not strictly operational to strengthen the institutional service, in recent years the following steps have been taken: •
• it is designed to support departments constantly in maximum operating conditions; • has an essentially static device; • is divided into just two levels - the General Command and the Regions/Schools/Btg. - without intermediate gear reduction mechanisms. The Interregional Commands/ Divisions/Brigades, in fact - in the more general framework of their command action - currently carry out exclusively coordination and logistical control functions in order to guarantee unity of direction.
In summary, the Army, like other Military Organizations, has moved from "just in case" logistics, i.e. based on the analysis of potential needs, with consequent burdensome and unused accumulation of materials to deal with any type of emergency, to "just in time" logistics, which, based on accurate planning and preventive programming activities, based on the determination of actual needs, provides what is needed when needed, minimizing inventories.

9.6.1.2 Ammunition As regards the definition of ammunition stocks, a complete review of the calculation criteria is underway within NATO. In particular, for war ammunition, and more specifically for those with a caliber greater than
20 mm, (defined as "battle-decisive ammunition"), the calculation to quantify stocks is no longer linked to the well-known "day of fire" parameters or “combat life” and does not take into account any time factors.
Publications Center for Peace Studies www.studiperlapace.it
9.6.1 Land segment 9.6.1.1 Spare Parts The almost complete "outsourcing" of the maintenance of the FA's commercial vehicle fleet has made it possible to achieve almost zero spare parts stocks for this type of vehicle - except for consumables ( batteries, covers, windshield wiper blades, etc.) - for which the minimum level of autonomy has been maintained, by virtue of their ready availability on the market.
However, stocks of spare parts are maintained for combat, tactical and tactical-logistic vehicles not of commercial origin, taking into account the time needed by the industry for their production. For these means, even if the need has decreased compared to the past, a quantitative and qualitative deficiency must still be complained about. ____________ This ammunition, in fact, is calculated according to the "objective-oriented" methodology, which, using a mathematical model, allows the stocks to be calculated in terms of the number of shots necessary to destroy the objectives envisaged in the different planning hypotheses (Planning Situations). , of which the "most expensive" one is taken as a reference for the constitution of the stocks itself. The results of this procedure, currently being refined, must be verified in terms of their financial feasibility and adequate storage possibilities.
However, as far as training ammunition is concemed, the stocks are calculated taking into account that the quantities to be acquired must not exceed the maximum numerical values that can be consumed in the period equal to the validity of the ammunition itself or, in the case of long durations, for its life. technique of the weapon system that uses it. These calculations also consider the "additions" that the training stocks receive from war ammunition close to the limit of their technical life, as part of a correct rotation. In this regard, it should be noted that the release for consumption of war ammunition that would otherwise be destined for disuse is undoubtedly one of the most sensitive problems for the purposes of economic management of stocks, also to avoid subsequent costly disposals.

9.8 Movement and Transport Policy The new strategic functions of the sector impose, especially as regards the land instrument, an accentuated mobility of forces not only in the tactical field, but also and above all at a strategic level. In this context, maritime and air transport means are of particular importance.
9.7 Military Healthcare Policy The sector, currently in considerable difficulty due to a significant exodus of highly specialized personnel, requires an urgent, substantial and radical review. The reference model on which it is currently structured, adapted to Armed Forces of considerable size and mainly sedentary, is in stark contrast with the current needs for strong mobility and streamlining dictated by the new NATO doctrine. From this perspective, the Defense has set itself the objective of interforce harmonization of Military Health, through the establishment of an ad hoc "Task Force" for the identification of a new structure and an adequate regulatory instrument for its reorganization. This new structure should solve the current problems of the sector, currently rather fragmented and with a management of resources that is inevitably not always cost-effective. ____________ exposed to risks and for those units destined for the Reaction Forces with "outside air" redeployments.
The Mobile Support Department was established with the aim of achieving STO capability on multiple bases Publications Center for Peace Studies www.studiperlapace.it The organizational structure includes, in addition to the Operational Technical Services Group, Operational Logistics Services Group, Road Transport Group, Administrative Service and the Defense Group, two Support Squadrons capable of supporting their own personnel and a contingent of equal size of combat forces, guaranteeing the essential services for carrying out the flight activity of the redeployed departments, supporting at least two contemporary needs, as is the case at the moment, which sees the Armed Force busy with the redeployment of Dakovica and Pristina.
The organizational structure of the Department was designed to carry out the assigned tasks and in compliance with the principles deriving from the new doctrine which see the concepts of flexibility, mobility, standardization and sustainability even more established.
9.6.3.3 The Mobile Support Department In line with NATO dictates, which required the AM Units to have a complete Survive To Operate (STO) capability, in a context of operations supported both by the permanent locations and by any redeployment locations, the Aeronautica Militare has equipped itself with a Mobile Support Department (RMS) which is based at Villafranca Airport in Verona.

9.10 Clothing and Equipment Policy Always in compliance with the general spirit of rationalization and optimization of the logistics sector, in particular, a significant revision work has been started in the clothing and equipment sector Only a limited number of warehouses will be maintained for the supply of Naval Units.
And it is precisely in relation to the problems related to strategic transport that, given the very high cost of the machines, two options are being evaluated, namely, direct acquisition or participation in the creation of a pool of aircraft within the Alliance for common use.
Defense has therefore started actions to renew the fleet, with particular attention to the logistical sustainability of the new flight lines to be acquired. For the Transport line, the decision to modemize it with the acquisition of C130J and C27J aircraft is now consolidated while activities are underway relating to the replacement of the B707 in-flight refueling aircraft with modem B767s which would, at the same time, also guarantee a good strategic transportation capability.

9.9 Provisioning Policy In this vital and delicate sector, the implementation of a new policy aimed at almost completely outsourcing this service has recently begun, which will lead, in particular, to: • entrusting the food supply and provisioning service of the Defense sector to specialized sectors of "Large Retail", obviously with the necessary clauses to safeguard the needs and safety of the military;
As regards air transport, the current fleet, technologically dated, does not allow the air transport of some types of armored vehicles whose immediate availability is of particular importance in operational theatres.
For transport by sea, the Defense has evaluated the feasibility/convenience of purchasing naval transport units of the military Ro-Ro type. In this regard, after careful analysis, we have come to the conclusion that, although without completely excluding the use of such a carrier in the future, the most economically advantageous solution is the use of the rental of merchant vessels, seeking, however, contractual forms - exclusive use - who ensure said service at any time and condition. In this context, it should also be remembered that Defense has launched a study in order to identify a regulatory instrument that allows, in particular situations of national/intemational crisis, the acquisition of civil/merchant transport structures for the mobility needs of military forces engaged in Crisis Response Operations (CROs).
However, there remains the need to bring together at the level of the Defense General Staff, in a single Movement and Transport Office, all the organizational skills for the movement of troops and materials. • ____________

The main objective of the aforementioned provisions of the Law was to find financial resources using the assets in use by the Defense, but no longer necessary or responsive to modem needs, to allocate them to the Defense itself, to compensate for the reductions made to its budget following the difficult economic situation of public finances. Added to this were the secondary, but no less important, objectives relating to the rationalization of the military presence in the territory and a more profitable reuse of state property with great urban and social relevance, which can be achieved through exchanges. be reassigned, within the overall limit of 1400 billion lire, to the estimate of the Ministry of Defense, upon proposal of the same, for the achievement of the objectives of modemization and operational and infrastructural strengthening of the Armed Forces.

And precisely due to the breadth of the subject - which involves branches of various nature (military policy, operations, logistics, financial planning, infrastructures, telecommunications and command and control systems) - the staff action is difficult and laborious as it requires a close coordination between numerous organizational elements of the CEO, whose structure is not designed for unitary management of the programs.
The Defense, in addition to the properties listed in the aforementioned Legislative Decree, has also taken steps through the Ministry of Finance, Department of Territory, to transfer the works of the former permanent fortification to the autonomous provinces of Trento and Bolzano.
The transfer of the aforementioned properties takes place with very simple methods, through the signing of the relevant delivery reports - by the representatives of the Ministry of Finance/Department of Land, the Ministry of Defense and the provinces involved which give the right for the registration and transfer of the properties in question in favor of the provinces themselves. The transfer operations have mostly already been completed, except for a very few properties for which they are still underway.
Despite the scale of the problem, the NATO Resources sector has so far been considered atypical, limiting knowledge to a few "insiders". On the contrary, resource issues require the adoption of national positions that often go beyond the technical-military aspects to directly affect political decisions.

In the 1990s, in order to optimize the allocation of joint military financial resources, member countries strengthened NATO's management structure, promoting the development of targeted structures and establishing a high-level resources commission: the Senior Resource Board (SRB), responsible for the overall management of NATO's military resources.

14 Defense bodies involved in the programs in question.

The Defense General Staff, to carry out the tasks connected with this intervention, has set up its own local office called PA - 2000 which, included in the Joint Project Coordination Office, constitutes the natural interface between Italy and NATO.
The Memorandum of Understanding (MOU) between NATO and Italy has been signed, and concrete implementation of the project is underway.
The sector policy has as its objectives the search for optimal conditions for maintaining, rationalizing and strengthening structures to make them functional and compliant with actual needs, developing housing standards based on the optimization of the cost/ effectiveness ratio.
10.6 Infrastructural problems of the Carabinieri Corps 10.6.1 Introduction The buildings, plants and structures constitute the logistical-infrastructural complex necessary to satisfy the needs of the different organizational lines into which the Corps is divided, configuring itself, due to the consequences it determines on the operation and efficiency of the Departments, as an essential component of the command organisation.
The choice of the Lago Patria site (Giugliano - Naples) was made on the basis of the operational requirements established by NATO and the availability of state-owned areas, through a complex feasibility study.
AFSOUTH is currently located in Bagnoli, in properties rented by the Banco di Napoli Foundation. The relocation and construction project of the new AFSOUTH headquarters was decided and planned by the Atlantic Alliance for an estimated cost of approximately
300 billion lire.
At the end of 1999, after having obtained NATO funding of around 60 billion lire, the Defense General Staff began a calm dialogue with the Mayor of Verona to identify altemative solutions, given that the neighboring University had insistently requested that the barracks entered into its infrastructural assets. A Declaration of Intent was signed with the Municipality, which provides for an exchange between Passalacqua and an area of approximately 30 hectares in the southem outskirts of the city, called "La Genovesa", capable of also hosting the Land Operational Forces Command . 11.1.1 General information The competitions provided by the Armed Forces are subordinated to their institutional priority commitments. They consist in the use of personnel, means and materials in tasks that are the same or similar to those normally carried out within the Military Organization. The role of the Armed Forces is to be considered complementary to that played by the Dicasteries and civil organizations institutionally responsible for the various sectors of intervention. ____________
• Law 382/1978 "Principle rules on military discipline" which, in defining the competences of the Armed Forces, establishes that the task of the Army, Navy and Air Force is also to contribute to the protection of free institutions and to the good of the national community in cases of public disasters;
• the Circ. SMD-G-006 "Directives for military competitions in peacetime" ed. 1991, which defines the methods of intervention, the rules to be followed, specifies the limits of the delegation of the Minister's Cabinet to the MS of FA and reports the methods for reimbursement of financial charges;

In particular: the forecasting activity, carried out through the issuing of a daily meteorological forecast and snow assessment bulletin; teaching, management and control activities in the sector of safety and rescue in the winter mountains and on the ski slopes; training activities, such as: qualification course for meteorological observers for the staff of the Alpine Troops and the State Forestry Corps; course for rescuers on ski slopes for the staff of the Alpine Troops and the State Forestry Corps; course on avalanche detection and prediction techniques for foreign military personnel; research, studies and experiences relating to the environment and phenomena connected with snow and avalanches; participation of managers in conferences and seminars, in Italy and abroad, of interest in the specific sector; production and updating of Military Avalanche Cartography and special cartography (altimetric, clivometric and exposure maps); collaboration and participation in the activities carried out by the bodies responsible for Civil Protection, the environment and regional and provincial services; meteorological support at home and abroad for the units engaged in training/operational activities.
The Service is currently based on the following constituent bodies: o the Alpine Troop Command in Bolzano, with managerial, organizational, coordination and the Universities, such as the Faculty of Agriculture of Turin and the Faculty of Motor Sciences of the University of Perugia with which some educational collaborations have been started. • o “Meteomont” pursues its objectives through the development of multiple activities, among them for all those who live in the mountain environment or frequent it for work and/or tourism reasons. or the Data Collection Center at the Air Force Meteorological Center of Milan-Linate, for the collection, compilation and distribution of meteorological information, based on the data provided by the Air Force Meteorological Service.

11.1.4.2 Navy The functions carried out by the Navy within the framework of the relationship between the Armed Forces and the country derive from the needs of public security, environmental protection, protection of economic activities at sea and public utility, with the use of the potential of military structures .
An index of the considerable interest aroused by the activities carried out by the Meteomont Service is given by the number of contacts on the relevant website (www.meteomont.it) which during the winter season reaches up to
500 contacts/day.
In relation to the evolution taking place in the Armed Forces and the intemational missions in progress, the criteria followed for the granting of competitions - without prejudice to the maximum availability ensured within the limits of available personnel and material resources - will increasingly tend to favor the use of units with a strong specialist connotation (engineers and helicopters) which are normally not available in the civilian sector. firefighting with helicopters, medical and traumatized transport with helicopters, the decommissioning of unexploded war remnants by specialist units, the hydrographic service and the Lighthouses and Maritime Signals service.
The validity of the safety and prevention system put in place by the Meteomont Service has been confirmed in recent years, especially in the civil world, with the start of numerous collaborations with public and private bodies and various information bodies including RAI, RAI -CCISS and numerous periodical magazines (Airone, Rivista della Montagna, etc.).
Other significant interventions in favor of the community also concem the water supply to the smaller islands (approximately 200,000 cubic meters per year), rescue and assistance during natural disasters, the summary of competitions provided by the army ____________ As part of the general maritime police activities, the MM carries out control activities on merchant shipping, including visits and inspections of documentation and cargo.
Conclusions The FA cannot, due to priority operational needs, bind itself to preventive commitments, without prejudice to its availability to intervene with the adherence allowed by its device and with qualified modules within the feasibility limits imposed by the equipment supplied and the procedures in force .
Among these functions, those with a strong operational connotation concem general surveillance and control at sea, the control of clandestine immigration flows (through devices in the waters of the lower Adriatic and the Otranto Channel, in the Northem Ionian Sea and in the Sicilian Channel ), the supervision of economic activities and the protection of fishing freedom (through the surveillance and protection of national fishing vessels operating in the critical areas of the Sicilian channel and the Adriatic Sea), search and rescue at sea (intervening with its own " guard units for saving human lives at sea", especially in complex operations on the high seas also with the use of offshore patrol vessels equipped with the capacity for the maritime rescue service), the protection and control of underwater archaeological assets (through the use of specialized means to support activities to safeguard the maritime environmental heritage and archaeological research), the protection of the marine environment and the fight against marine pollution.
Publications Center for Peace Studies www.studiperlapace.it The maintenance and development of the aforementioned collaborations, the scientific and technological progress in the meteorological field and the progressive transition from a conscript Army to an Army on a voluntary basis, with consequent lower availability and high cost of human resources, today require a substantial updating and strengthening of the systems for acquiring, transmitting, archiving and processing the meteorological data that make up the "Meteomont system", according to new criteria, such as: • updating the software and hardware currently making up the system; • transition from the current data reception and transmission system, via radio/wire, to more efficient and economical systems (e.g. satellite and digital); • the replacement of the fixed manual detection network with new automatic stations, providing for an enhancement of the current automatic network by 50%, equal to 24 stations, with the consequent substantial reduction of the staff employed by approximately 60%.

Finally, it is worth remembering the complex of traditional supports provided by the AM, both to the country and to other public administration departments, with the carrying out of activities relating to ATS (Air Traffic Services) services rendered to civil and the weather service. This specific task is carried out with seamless activities throughout the day through a widespread network of meteorological detection stations and processing and forecasts by the General Meteorology Office. This specific activity also involves the carrying out of medical flights and, more generally, the broad-spectrum rescue activity for the benefit of populations in the event of public disasters and emergencies. This function with an eminent operational characteristic is also carried out in favor and in support of the Public Security authorities such as the FA's contribution provided previously and during the recent G-8 Summit in Genoa. In this circumstance, in fact, the FA ensured the creation of an "umbrella" to protect the entire area affected by the event from the aerial threat.

• The Subgroup for the development of environmental protection in military installations has produced, in cooperation with the USA, a questionnaire for the cognitive reconnaissance of all the activities taking place in military bases for the purpose of identifying any polluted sites and preventing pollution. pollution. This questionnaire is being distributed to all peripheral defense bodies as an aid in identifying polluted sites, in implementation of Art. 17 of the Environment Ministerial Decree n.22/97.
To confirm the importance attributed to environmental protection issues, the Defense General Staff felt the need to raise the Environment Section to Office level by inserting the treatment of accident prevention matters which are similar in some respects.
11.2.2.1 Establishment of the accident prevention and environment office The first step was the creation of a Regulatory and Environment Section which was initially set up to address all those environmental impact problems linked to military infrastructures. As the activities progressed, the Section became the coordination point for all aspects of environmental protection within the Defense sector.
The Subgroup for noise protection is developing an implementing regulation of the framework law on noise pollution (L. 447/95), relating to the noise produced by military aircraft. By virtue of the experience gained by the Air Force with the MILNOISE project and with the creation of a mobile vehicle for noise detection (total cost of the project 1 billion), the Subgroup is providing assistance to local authorities for the purposes of noise mapping provided for by the aforementioned law.
The Subgroup for the protection from electromagnetic pollution to address the buming problem of non-ionizing radiation. Also in anticipation of the future approval of a framework law on the subject, the Subgroup has already produced a first draft of the "Joint Regulation for the Protection from Non-Ionizing Radiation", which will be further improved to include the provisions of Legislative Decree 626/94 and subsequent modifications.
However, the development of such a program requires financial and personnel resources that are not immediately available.
Furthermore, the Defense General Staff has planned a series of actions, with the intention of implementing them together with the activities within NATO and within the Bilateral Cooperation with the United States of America: • establishment of an Accident Prevention and Environment Office ; • establishment of a joint working group for environmental protection; • development of an Environmental Policy of the Ministry of Defence; • definition of a Joint Environmental Directive; • staff training and training; • determination of the organization. •

Consequently, since the Armed Force must ensure the indispensable activity of combat aircraft necessary for the achievement and maintenance of an adequate level of training, actions have been initiated and contacts have been made with foreign countries aimed at carrying out low-altitude flight activities in environments and territories not subject to limitations.
These limitations have, in fact, decreased the areas available and increased the minimum quotas, making the BBQ activity severely penalized.
For years the Air Force has provided its contribution with aircraft and personnel to safeguard the forestry heritage.
This activity began in 1978, with the C130 aircraft of the 46th Air Brigade and, subsequently, the support was integrated by G222 aircraft of the same brigade, by the CL215 "Canadair" and by the HH3F helicopters of the 15th Wing. In 1987, the Canadairs were retumed to the Ministry of Civil Protection. • Again in order to contain the noise produced by military aircraft during training activities within acceptable limits, the Air Force has imposed limitations on very low altitude (BBQ) flights on national territory.

The Italian Armed Forces find themselves having to deal with a tradition of prejudicial distrust towards the military institution and the military function itself. But, at the same time, the new tasks, ranging from immigration control to peace-keeping missions, have induced profound changes and given the military world a strong visibility. The new commitments of the Armed Forces create the conditions for a different consideration, also by the press which is particularly interested in military missions abroad.
